Praise be to Allah, Lord of all worlds. If the man being pursued belonged to the corrupt, oppressive faction who had renounced obedience, broken away from the Muslim community, and unlawfully attacked Muslims’ lives and property, and they were being pursued so that the command of Allah and His Messenger might be enforced upon them, then it is permissible to fight the one among them who turned back to fight. Whoever killed him in the circumstances described bears no liability. Indeed, in the case of those who wage war, the same ruling applies to an accomplice as to a direct perpetrator according to the majority of leading scholars, including Abū Hanīfah, Mālik, and Ahmad. Whoever assists them is subject to the same ruling as they are.

---

## Page 16535

He—may Allah Most High have mercy on him—was asked:

“Concerning the ‘brotherhood’ practised by some people nowadays, in which each pledges himself by saying, ‘My wealth is your wealth, my blood is your blood, and my child is your child,’ and the other says the same, and one drinks the other’s blood: is this practice religiously sanctioned or not? If it is not religiously sanctioned and commendable, is it nevertheless permissible? Does it give rise to any of the rulings of Islamic law that apply to actual brothers? What is the meaning of the brotherhood that the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, established between the Emigrants and the Helpers?”

He answered:

Praise be to Allah, Lord of all worlds. This practice, in the form described, is not religiously sanctioned, by agreement of the Muslims. The original brotherhood was that established by the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, between the Emigrants and the Helpers. He bound them together in an alliance in the house of Anas ibn Mālik. For example, he established brotherhood between Sa‘d ibn al-Rabī‘ and ‘Abd al-Rahmān ibn ‘Awf, so that Sa‘d said to ‘Abd al-Rahmān, “Take half my wealth, and choose either of my two wives so that I may divorce her and you may marry her.” ‘Abd al-Rahmān replied, “May Allah bless your wealth and your family. Show me the way to the market.” Likewise, he established brotherhood between Salmān al-Fārisī and Abū al-Dardā’. All of this is recorded in the Sahīh.

---

## Page 16536

As for the claims made by some authors writing on the Prophet’s biography that the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, established brotherhood between ‘Alī and Abū Bakr, and similar accounts, these are false by agreement of those versed in his hadith. He did not establish brotherhood between one Emigrant and another, or between one Helper and another; rather, he established it between the Emigrants and the Helpers. Through this brotherhood and alliance, they inherited from one another to the exclusion of their relatives, until Allah Most High revealed:

“But blood relatives have a stronger claim upon one another in the Book of Allah.”

Inheritance thus came to be based on kinship rather than this brotherhood and alliance. The scholars differed over whether such an alliance and brotherhood confer inheritance rights when there are no heirs through kinship or ties of manumission. There are two views:

The first is that they do confer inheritance rights. This is the position of Abū Hanīfah and of Ahmad according to one of the two reports from him, based on the words of Allah Most High:

“And those with whom you have made a pledge—give them their share.”

The second is that they confer no inheritance rights under any circumstances. This is the position of Mālik, al-Shāfi‘ī, and Ahmad according to the report well known among his followers. Those holding this view say that this verse has been abrogated.

People likewise differed over whether Islam sanctions two people establishing brotherhood and an alliance, as the Emigrants and the Helpers did. One view is that this has been abrogated, on account of what Muslim narrated in his Sahīh from Jābir: that the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, said:

“There is no alliance in Islam, and whatever alliance existed in the pre-Islamic period, Islam has only strengthened it.”

Another reason is that Allah has already made the believers brothers by the explicit wording of the Quran, and the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, said: “A Muslim is the brother of

---

## Page 16537

a Muslim; he neither forsakes him nor wrongs him. By Him in whose hand is my soul, none of you truly believes until he loves for his brother the good that he loves for himself.”

Whoever fulfils the obligations of faith is a brother to every believer, and every believer must uphold his rights, even if no special pact has been concluded between them. For Allah and His Messenger have already established brotherhood between them through His words:

“The believers are but brothers.”

The Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, said:

“I wish I could have seen my brothers.”

Anyone who remains entitled to the rights of faith must be treated accordingly: he should be praised for his good deeds and shown loyalty on their account, admonished against his evil deeds and shunned on their account as far as possible. The Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, said:

“Support your brother, whether he is an oppressor or is oppressed.” I said, “O Messenger of Allah, I support him when he is oppressed, but how do I support him when he is an oppressor?” He said, “You prevent him from committing oppression; that is how you support him.”

Every Muslim must ensure that his love and hatred, his loyalty and enmity, follow the command of Allah and His Messenger. He must love what Allah and His Messenger love, hate what Allah and His Messenger hate, be loyal to those who are loyal to Allah and His Messenger, and oppose those who oppose Allah and His Messenger.

Anyone who has both good deeds that warrant loyalty and evil deeds that warrant opposition must be treated accordingly. Such is the case with sinful Muslims: they deserve reward and punishment, loyalty and opposition, love and hatred, according to the righteousness and wickedness within them. For:

“Whoever does an atom’s weight of good will see it.”

“And whoever does an atom’s weight of evil will see it.”

---

## Page 16538

And this is the position of Ahl al-Sunnah wa-l-Jamā‘ah, in contrast to the Khawārij and Mu‘tazilah, and likewise to the Murji’ah and Jahmiyyah. The former incline to one side and the latter to the other, while Ahl al-Sunnah wa-l-Jamā‘ah occupy the middle ground.

Some people hold that such bonds of brotherhood and alliance are sanctioned. This accords with the view that an alliance establishes mutual inheritance rights. Yet Muslims do not dispute that the child of either party does not become the other’s child, inheriting from him alongside his children. Allah, glorified be He, abolished the adoption practised during the pre-Islamic era, whereby a man would adopt another man’s child as his own. Allah Most High said:

“Allah has not placed two hearts within any man’s body, nor has He made your wives whom you declare to be like your mothers your mothers, nor has He made your adopted sons your own sons.”

He also said:

“Call them by their fathers’ names; that is more just in Allah’s sight. If you do not know their fathers, then they are your brothers in faith.”

Likewise, neither party’s property becomes the other’s property, to be inherited from him; this cannot hold for both parties. However, if each willingly consents to the other’s use of his property, that is permissible, as was the practice of the early generations. One would enter the other’s home and eat his food in his absence, knowing that he willingly consented to this, as Allah Most High said:

“or your friend’s house.”

As for each drinking the other’s blood, this is impermissible under any circumstances. At the very least, besides the blood’s impurity, it entails imitating two people who form a bond of brotherhood to help one another in sin and transgression,

---

## Page 16539

whether through acts of sexual immorality or satanic love, such as love for beardless youths and the like—even if they present it otherwise, as a partnership in trades or similar pursuits—or through cooperation in wronging others and consuming people’s wealth unjustly. This resembles the bonds that some who claim to be spiritual masters and followers of the spiritual path establish with women: one of them forms such a bond with a woman unrelated to him and spends time alone with her. Groups among them have admitted to the acts of sexual immorality that occur between them.

Such bonds, and any similar arrangements involving cooperation in anything Allah has forbidden, whatever it may be, are prohibited by Muslim consensus. The disagreement concerns only a bond intended to foster cooperation in righteousness and piety, such that obedience to Allah unites the two parties and disobedience to Allah separates them. As they say: “The Sunnah unites us, and innovation divides us.” It is this form that is disputed.

Most scholars do not endorse it, regarding the brotherhood of faith established by Allah and His Messenger as sufficient, for that brotherhood suffices and secures every good. One should therefore strive to fulfil its obligations properly, since Allah has given believers rights over one another that exceed what people themselves seek. Other scholars have permitted such a bond in a lawful form, provided it contains nothing contrary to Islamic law.

But if it is declared to entail sharing in good deeds and sins, so that whichever of the two enters Paradise will bring his companion into it, or other such conditions that they may impose upon one another, these conditions and others like them are invalid and cannot be fulfilled, for intercession cannot take place

---

## Page 16540

except by Allah’s permission. Allah knows best what their circumstances will be and what each of them deserves. How, then, can a Muslim be bound to do something beyond his power, when neither his own state in this matter nor that of the other person is known? Thus, we find that those who impose these conditions do not understand what they are stipulating. Were one of them to realize that some of his wealth would be taken from him in this world, Allah knows best whether he would agree to them or not!

In short, all stipulations, contracts, and covenants that people enter into, whether concerning brotherhood or otherwise, must be referred back to Allah’s Book and the Sunnah of His Messenger. Every stipulation that accords with the Book and the Sunnah must be fulfilled, and:

“Any stipulation someone makes that is not in Allah’s Book is invalid, even if there are a hundred such stipulations. Allah’s Book has greater authority, and His stipulation is more binding.”

Whenever a stipulation conflicts with what Allah and His Messenger have stipulated, it is invalid. Examples include stipulating that someone else’s child should be one’s own son; that one should have patronage over someone emancipated by another; that one’s son or relative should not inherit from one; that one will assist another in everything he wants and support him against every enemy, whether he is in the right or in the wrong; that one will obey him in everything he commands; or that one will admit him to Paradise and save him from the Fire unconditionally, and other such stipulations.

When such stipulations are made, those that accord with what Allah and His Messenger have commanded must be fulfilled, while those that entail what Allah and His Messenger have forbidden must not be fulfilled. This is agreed upon among Muslims. As for stipulations concerning permissible matters, there is disagreement and further detail, which this is not the place to discuss.

---

## Page 16541

The same applies to stipulations in sales, gifts, endowments, and vows; pledges of allegiance to rulers; covenants with shaykhs; covenants between those who enter into brotherhood; agreements among members of lineages and tribes; and similar matters. Everyone must obey Allah and His Messenger in all things and avoid disobeying Allah and His Messenger in anything. No created being may be obeyed in disobedience to the Creator. Allah and His Messenger must be dearer to a person than everything else, and he must obey only those who believe in Allah and His Messenger. And Allah knows best.

---

## Page 16542

Chapter: The Ruling on the Apostate

Shaykh al-Islām—may Allah be pleased with him—was asked:

Two men discussed “the issue of pollination.” One of them said: “Whoever disparages the Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, or says something implying disparagement of the Messenger, has disbelieved. However, declaring unbelief in general terms does not entail declaring a particular individual an unbeliever. A scholar may address an issue according to his independent scholarly judgment and err without thereby being declared an unbeliever, even though someone making that statement may be declared an unbeliever if the proof warranting that judgment has been established against him. If we were to declare every scholar an unbeliever on such grounds, we would have to declare so-and-so an unbeliever”—and he named one of the well-known scholars who do not deserve to be declared unbelievers, namely al-Ghazālī—“for he stated in one of his books that the Messenger had erred in the matter of pollinating date palms.”

Does this constitute disparagement of the Messenger in any way? Is he liable to discretionary punishment for absolving scholars of unbelief when they say such things, or not?

If this is reported, but he cannot at the time obtain the actual book from which he quoted it, though he is known to be truthful, is he liable to discretionary punishment for that, or not? Does this apply whether he was correct or mistaken in reporting the scholar’s words? Does this constitute disparagement of the Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace?

If someone wrongs such a person or accuses him of disparaging the Messenger or the scholars, and seeks to have him punished for that, what is incumbent upon him? Give us a ruling; may you be rewarded.

---

## Page 16543

He replied:

Praise be to Allah. According to the agreement of Muslim scholars, these words contain no disparagement whatsoever of the Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, nor do they disparage Muslim scholars. Rather, their substance is reverence and respect for the Messenger, and an affirmation that nothing disparaging may be said about him. Indeed, the speaker made a general declaration that anyone who disparages the Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, or says something implying disparagement of him, has disbelieved. This strongly emphasizes reverence for him and the obligation to guard against words that imply any disparagement of him.

At the same time, he explained that Muslim scholars who speak in this world according to their independent scholarly judgment must not be declared unbelievers merely because of an error in what they say. This is a sound statement with which one must agree. Allowing ignorant people to declare Muslim scholars unbelievers is among the gravest wrongs. This practice originated with the Khawārij and Rāfidah, who declare Muslim leaders unbelievers because of what they believe to be their errors in religion.

Ahl al-Sunnah wa-l-Jamā‘ah are agreed that Muslim scholars must not be declared unbelievers merely for making an error. Rather, some statements of every person may be accepted and others rejected, except those of the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. Not everyone whose words are partly rejected because of an error is thereby to be declared an unbeliever or an evildoer; indeed, he may not even incur sin. For Allah Most High has related the believers’ supplication:

“Our Lord, do not hold us accountable if we forget or make a mistake.”

An authentic hadith reports from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, that Allah Most High said:

“I have done so.”

Muslim scholars are agreed that none of the Muslim scholars who differ over the infallibility of the prophets are to be declared unbelievers, nor are those who say that the prophets may commit minor sins and make mistakes, but are not allowed to persist

---

## Page 16544

in them. By Muslim consensus, none of these scholars is to be declared an unbeliever. For they maintain that the prophets are protected from being allowed to persist in such things. Were these scholars judged to be unbelievers, this would entail declaring many Shāfi‘īs, Mālikīs, Hanafīs, Hanbalīs, Ash‘arīs, scholars of hadith and Quranic commentary, and Sufis to be unbelievers, although Muslims agree that they are not. Indeed, the leading authorities among these groups hold this view.

The view he attributed to Shaykh Abū Hāmid al-Ghazālī was also expressed by leading Shāfi‘ī jurists qualified to derive rulings within the school, whose standing in the Shāfi‘ī school exceeds that of Abū Hāmid. Thus Shaykh Abū Hāmid al-Isfarāyīnī, the school’s foremost authority after al-Shāfi‘ī and Ibn Surayj, said in his commentary:

“Our position is that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, may make mistakes just as we may. The difference between us, however, is that we may be left in error, whereas the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, is not left in error. His lapses of memory serve only to establish the Sunnah. It is reported that he said: ‘I only forget in order to establish the Sunnah for you.’”

This question was discussed in the field of legal theory by this Shaykh Abū Hāmid, Abū al-Tayyib al-Tabarī, and Shaykh Abū Ishāq al-Shīrāzī. It was likewise discussed by the other scholarly groups among the followers of Mālik, al-Shāfi‘ī, Ahmad, and Abū Hanīfah. Some claimed that the early generations were unanimously agreed upon this view, as has been related from Abū Sulaymān al-Khattābī and others like him.

Nevertheless, Muslims agree that none of these leading scholars is to be declared an unbeliever. Whoever declares them unbelievers on this account deserves a severe punishment that will deter him

---

## Page 16545

and others like him from declaring Muslims unbelievers. In such matters, one should simply say that their view is correct or mistaken. Those who agree with them say that their view is correct; those who dispute it say that it is mistaken and that their opponents’ view is correct.

The words of the man in question indicate that he does not agree with them on this matter, but does reject declaring them unbelievers. Anyone who wrongs such a person by accusing him of disparaging the Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, or the scholars must be punished, for he explicitly states the opposite of both accusations.

Qādī ‘Iyād discussed this issue, although he was among the most emphatic proponents of prophetic protection from sin and error. He divided the discussion of this subject into categories, eventually saying:

“The seventh category is to mention something that may occur on the part of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, but concerning which there is disagreement as to whether he may be allowed to persist in it, as well as the human experiences that may befall him and may be attributed to him. Or one may mention the trials he endured patiently for Allah’s sake despite their severity: his struggles with his enemies and the harm they inflicted upon him. This also includes recounting his early circumstances and life, the hardships he encountered in his time, and the difficulties he endured in earning his livelihood. All this is done by way of transmitting reports, scholarly discussion, and understanding the established scope of the prophets’ protection from sin and error and what may befall them.”

He said:

“This category falls outside the preceding six. It contains no disparagement, belittlement, denigration or disrespect, either in the apparent wording or in the speaker’s intention. However, such discussion must take place with scholars and students of religion who understand its purposes and properly grasp its benefits. It should be kept from anyone who might not understand it, or for whom it is feared that it might cause confusion in matters of faith.”

---

## Page 16546

Qādī ‘Iyād had earlier discussed the case of someone who utters an insult while quoting another person or transmitting it from someone else. He said:

“In this case, one must consider how he relates it and the circumstances surrounding his words. The ruling varies accordingly, taking one of four forms: obligatory, recommended, disliked or prohibited.”

He then explained that such reporting is permissible when offered as testimony or for a similar purpose involving the application of the prescribed legal ruling to the speaker, or to expose the vileness and failings of the person who uttered it—as opposed to relating it for neither of these purposes. He said:

“No one may make sport of the honor of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, or bandy about words disparaging him, whether recounting them or transmitting them from others, without a permissible purpose recognized by Islamic law.”

Qādī ‘Iyād’s words thus make clear that what the man in question said does not fall into this category, since it concerns matters of scholarly disagreement. They also make clear that no one may relate anything that does fall into this category without a permissible purpose recognized by Islamic law.

The man in question raised this point only to defend al-Ghazālī and other Muslim scholars like him against being declared unbelievers. It is well known that preventing the Muslim scholars who discussed this subject from being declared unbelievers—indeed, defending Muslim scholars against such accusations even when they have erred—is among the most rightful purposes recognized by Islamic law. Even supposing that someone defends a speaker against being declared an unbeliever because he believes him not to be one, seeking to protect him and support his Muslim brother, this would be a worthy purpose recognized by Islamic law. If he exercises his judgment in this and is correct, he receives two rewards; if he exercises his judgment and errs, he receives one reward.

---

## Page 16547

Thus, in any case, this speaker is to be commended for what he did and will receive reward and recompense for it, provided his intention was good. The person who objects to his action is more deserving of discretionary punishment than he is, for the objector’s words entail impugning Muslim scholars by accusing them of unbelief. Clearly, the former is more deserving of discretionary punishment than the latter, if either deserves such punishment. If, however, each is exercising legitimate scholarly judgment, seeking to obey Allah and His Messenger to the best of his ability, neither incurs any sin.

Whether the speaker was accurate or mistaken in reporting this, it involves no disparagement of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. The same applies whether or not he produces the cited passage, for producing it serves no purpose: Muslim scholars whose numbers Allah Most High alone knows have expressed views like the one he reported from al-Ghazālī. Some of them are greater scholars than al-Ghazālī, while others are beneath him. Whoever declares these scholars unbelievers deserves punishment by the agreement of the Muslims. Indeed, most Muslim scholars and the majority of the early generations express the same view—even the dialectical theologians.

Abū al-Hasan al-Ashʿarī stated that most Ashʿarīs and Muʿtazilīs hold this view. He mentioned it in Usūl al-Fiqh (Principles of Jurisprudence), as did his associate Abū ʿAmr ibn al-Hājib. They regard this question as a matter of probable rather than certain knowledge, as the master Abū al-Maʿālī, Abū al-Hasan al-Āmidī and others explicitly stated. How, then, can Muslim scholars be declared unbelievers over matters of uncertainty? Or how can the majority of Muslim scholars—or the majority of the early imams and the foremost scholars—be declared unbelievers without any proof whatsoever? Allah Most High knows best.

---

## Page 16548

He—may Allah have mercy on him—was asked:

“What do the esteemed scholars, the imams of the religion—may Allah be pleased with them all—say concerning a man who said, ‘I bear witness that there is no god but Allah, and I bear witness that Muhammad is His servant and Messenger,’ yet did not pray or perform any of the obligatory duties, claiming that this would not harm him, that he would enter Paradise, and that his body was forbidden to the Fire?

“And concerning a man who says, ‘I ask Allah and you to fulfil my need’: is this statement false or not? Is it permissible to say this or not?”

He replied:

Praise be to Allah.

Whoever does not believe that the five daily prayers, the prescribed zakat, fasting the month of Ramadan, and pilgrimage to the Ancient House are obligatory, and does not regard as forbidden what Allah and His Messenger have forbidden of shameful deeds, injustice, associating partners with Allah, and falsehood, is an unbeliever and an apostate. He must be called upon to repent and, if he does not, is to be put to death, by agreement of the imams of the Muslims. Merely uttering the two testimonies of faith will not avail him.

If, however, he says, “I acknowledge that these duties are binding upon me, and I know that they are obligatory and that whoever neglects them deserves Allah’s condemnation and punishment, but I do not perform them,” then such a person, too, deserves punishment

---

## Page 16549

in this world and the Hereafter, by agreement of the Muslims. He must perform the five daily prayers, by agreement of the scholars.

Most scholars say that he is to be ordered to pray; if he does not pray, he is to be put to death. If he persists in denying their obligation until he is put to death, he is an unbeliever by agreement of the imams. His body is not to be washed, no funeral prayer is to be offered over him, and he is not to be buried in Muslim cemeteries.

Whoever says, “Everyone who utters the two testimonies of faith, even without performing the obligatory duties or avoiding forbidden acts, will enter Paradise, and none of them will be punished in the Fire,” is an unbeliever and an apostate. He must be called upon to repent; if he does not repent, he is to be put to death. Rather, those who utter the two testimonies of faith fall into “different categories.” Among them are hypocrites who will be in the lowest depths of the Fire, as Allah Most High says:

“Indeed, the hypocrites will be in the lowest depths of the Fire, and you will never find anyone to help them—except those who repent, mend their ways, hold fast to Allah, and devote their religion sincerely to Allah; those will be with the believers,” to the end of the verse.

Allah Most High also says:

“Indeed, the hypocrites seek to deceive Allah, but He outwits them. When they stand for prayer, they stand lazily,” to the end of the verse.

In Sahīh Muslim, the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—is reported to have said:

“That is the hypocrite’s prayer! That is the hypocrite’s prayer! That is the hypocrite’s prayer! He watches the sun until, when it is between the two horns of a devil, he rises and rushes through four units of prayer, remembering Allah only a little during them.”

The Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—thus explained that someone who delays the prayer and rushes through it is a hypocrite. What, then, of someone who does not pray at all? Allah Most High has said:

“So woe to those who pray, yet are heedless of their prayers—those who show off.”

The scholars said that those who are “heedless of their prayers” are those who delay them beyond their appointed times and those who neglect their obligatory elements. If these people who pray are threatened with woe, what, then, of someone who does not pray at all?

---

## Page 16550

It is established in the two Sahīhs that the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—“will recognize his community by the radiance of their faces and limbs from the traces of ablution.” This radiance belongs only to those who perform ablution and pray: their faces, hands, and feet become radiant through ablution, and they pray with radiant faces and limbs. Whoever neither performs ablution nor prays will have no such radiance. He will therefore lack the distinguishing mark of the Muslims—the Prophet’s identifying insignia, like the insignia by which a commander recognizes his men—and will not belong to the community of Muhammad—may Allah bless him and grant him peace.

It is also established in the Sahīh that:

“The Fire consumes every part of the son of Adam except the marks of prostration.”

Thus, whoever is not among those who prostrate to the One who is worshipped, the All-Forgiving, the Most Loving, the Lord of the Glorious Throne, will be consumed by the Fire.

In the Sahīh, the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—said:

“Between a servant and associating partners with Allah lies only the abandonment of prayer.”

He also said:

“The covenant that distinguishes us from them is prayer; whoever abandons it has disbelieved.”

And he said:

“The first of a servant’s deeds for which he will be called to account is prayer.”

A servant should not say, “Whatever Allah wills and so-and-so wills,” “I have none but Allah and so-and-so,” “I seek what I need from Allah and from so-and-so,” or similar expressions. Rather, he should say, “Whatever Allah wills, then whatever so-and-so wills,” and “I seek what I need from Allah, then from so-and-so.” As related in the hadith, the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—said:

“Do not say, ‘Whatever Allah wills and Muhammad wills.’ Rather, say, ‘Whatever Allah wills, then whatever Muhammad wills.’”

A man said to him, “Whatever Allah wills and you will.” He replied:

“Have you made me an equal to Allah? Rather, whatever Allah alone wills.”

Allah knows best. May Allah bless Muhammad.

---

## Page 16551

What do the esteemed scholars—may Allah be pleased with them—say concerning “al-Hallāj, al-Husayn ibn Mansūr”? Was he a man of consummate truthfulness or a heretic? Was he a pious friend of Allah who feared Him? Or did he possess a spiritual state bestowed by the Most Merciful? Or was he among those who practised sorcery and charlatanry? Was he executed for heresy in the presence of Muslim scholars, or was he killed unjustly? Give us your ruling; may you be rewarded.

Shaykh al-Islām Abū al-Abbās Taqī al-Dīn Ahmad ibn Abd al-Halīm ibn Abd al-Salām Ibn Taymiyyah—may Allah sanctify his soul—replied:

All praise is due to Allah, Lord of all worlds. Al-Hallāj was executed for heresy established against him both by his own admission and by other evidence. What was established against him warranted execution by the consensus of the Muslims. Whoever says that he was killed unjustly is either a hypocrite and heretic or an ignorant, misguided person. He was executed for the various forms of disbelief widely reported from him; even some of these warranted his execution, let alone all of them together.

He was not among Allah’s pious friends. Rather, he engaged in acts of worship, ascetic disciplines, and spiritual striving: some were satanic, some arose from his own soul, and some conformed to the revealed law in one respect but not another. Thus he mingled truth with falsehood. He had travelled to India, learned various forms of sorcery, and authored a well-known book on sorcery that remains in existence to this day. He also uttered satanic sayings and performed fraudulent feats of trickery.

---

## Page 16552

Scholars, both his contemporaries and those who transmitted their accounts, compiled reports about him in numerous historical works. Among them was Abū Alī al-Hatī, who mentioned him in “History of Baghdad.” The hadith master Abū Bakr al-Khatīb devoted a lengthy biographical entry to him in “History of Baghdad.” Abū Yūsuf al-Qazwīnī compiled a volume of reports about him, and Abū al-Faraj ibn al-Jawzī wrote a work concerning him entitled “Dispelling Contention over Reports about al-Hallāj.”

Abū Abd al-Rahmān al-Sulamī discussed him at length in his historical work, “Generations of the Sufis,” reporting that many of the masters censured him, denounced his conduct, and did not count him among the masters of the spiritual path. Most of them disparaged him. Among those who censured and disparaged him was Abū al-Qāsim al-Junayd. Al-Hallāj was not executed during al-Junayd’s lifetime, but after his death: al-Junayd died in 298 AH, whereas al-Hallāj was executed a few years after 300 AH.

He was brought to Baghdad riding a camel, with someone proclaiming about him: “This is the missionary of the Qarmatians!” He remained imprisoned for some time, until statements containing disbelief and heresy were found among his words, and he acknowledged them. For example, he stated in one of his books that anyone who missed Hajj could erect a structure in his home, circumambulate it as one circumambulates the Sacred House, and give thirty orphans a specified amount of charity; that would suffice in place of Hajj.

They asked him, “Did you say this?”

He replied, “Yes.”

They asked him, “What is your source for this?”

He replied, “Al-Hasan al-Basrī mentioned it in ‘The Book of Prayer.’”

The judge Abū Umar said to him, “You are lying, you heretic! I have read this book, and this is not in it.”

The vizier then asked them to testify to what they had heard and issue a ruling on the penalty he deserved. They agreed that he must be executed.

---

## Page 16553

But scholars hold two views concerning a heretic who outwardly professes repentance: should his repentance be accepted and his life spared? Or should he be executed because his sincerity cannot be known, since he has always made such outward professions?

One group ruled that he should be invited to repent and spared execution, while the majority ruled that he should be executed even if he professes repentance. If his repentance is sincere, it will benefit him before Allah, though he is executed in this world, and the prescribed punishment will purify him. Similarly, if adulterers, thieves, and others repent after their cases have been brought before the ruler, the prescribed punishment must still be carried out upon them. If they are sincere, their execution serves as an expiation for them; whoever falsely claims repentance is punished by his execution.

Thus, if al-Hallāj had inwardly repented by the time of his execution, Allah will benefit him through that repentance. If he was lying, however, he was executed as a disbeliever. No miraculous favors appeared at the time of his execution. Anyone who claims that his blood wrote Allah’s name on the ground, that fluid ceased to flow from his leg, or anything similar, is lying.

Only an ignorant person or a hypocrite recounts these things. They were fabricated by heretics and enemies of Islam so that one of them could say, “The law of Muhammad ibn Abd Allāh puts Allah’s friends to death,” and so that such ravings would be heard. Yet many prophets were killed, as were countless companions of theirs, Companions of our Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—Successors, and other righteous people whose number Allah alone knows. They were killed by the swords of evildoers, disbelievers, oppressors, and others, yet none of their blood wrote Allah’s name. Blood is also impure

---

## Page 16554

so it is not permissible to write the name of Allah Most High with it. Is al-Hallāj, then, better than these people, and is his blood purer than theirs? At the time of his execution, he became distraught and outwardly professed repentance and adherence to the Sunnah, but this was not accepted from him. Had he lived, many ignorant people would have been led astray by him, for he engaged in fraudulent trickery and satanic states. Thus, only those who venerate satanic, ego-driven, and fraudulent states venerate him. As for Allah’s friends who knew al-Hallāj’s true condition, not one of them venerates him. This is why al-Qushayrī did not include him among the shaykhs in his Epistle, although he quoted some of his sayings that he considered good. Shaykh Abū Yaʿqūb al-Nahrajūrī had married his daughter to him, but when he discovered his heresy, he separated her from him.

ʿAmr ibn ʿUthmān used to declare him a disbeliever, saying:

“I was with him when he heard someone reciting the Quran and said, ‘I can compose something like this Quran,’ or words to that effect.”

Before each group, he would present whatever would induce them to venerate him. Before the people of the Sunnah, he presented himself as a Sunni, and before the Shia, as a Shia. Sometimes he wore the clothing of ascetics, and sometimes that of soldiers. Among his “tricks” was that he would send one of his companions to a place in the wilderness to hide some fruit and sweets there, then bring a group of worldly-minded people close to

---

## Page 16555

that place and say to them, “What would you like me to bring you from this wilderness?” One of them would ask for fruit or sweets, and he would say, “Wait here.” Then he would go to that place and bring back what he had hidden, or some of it. Those present would imagine that this was a miracle granted to him. He practised sorcery, and devils would sometimes serve him. On one occasion, some people were with him on Mount Abū Qubays and asked him for sweets. He went somewhere nearby and returned with a plate of sweets. They investigated the matter and discovered that these had been stolen from a confectioner’s shop in Yemen: a devil had carried them from that place.

Such things frequently happen to people other than al-Hallāj who experience satanic states. We know many such people, both in our own time and in other times. One example is a man now in Damascus whom a devil used to carry from the mountain of al-Sālihiyya to a village near Damascus. He would come through the air to the window of a house where people were gathered and enter while they watched. At night, he would come to “Bāb al-Saghīr” and pass through it with his companions. He was among the most wicked of people.

Another man was in al-Shuwayk, in a village called “al-Shāhida.” He would fly through the air to the mountaintop while people watched; a devil was carrying him. He was also a highway robber. Most of these people are shaykhs of evil. One such person is called “al-Bawway,” meaning the wicked one. They stage performances for him on a dark night and make bread as offerings. They do not mention Allah, nor do they allow anyone who mentions Allah, or any book containing mention of Allah, to be present. Then that man ascends

---

## Page 16556

—al-Bawā’—into the air while they watch. They hear him speaking to the devil and the devil speaking to him. Anyone who laughs or chokes on the bread is struck by the tambourine, although they cannot see who is wielding it. The devil then tells them some of what they ask about and orders them to offer him cattle, horses, and other animals, strangling them without mentioning Allah’s name over them. When they do so, he fulfils their needs.

Another shaykh admitted that he used to commit fornication with women and sodomize boys known as “al-hiwārāt.” He used to say: “A black dog with two white spots between its eyes comes to me and says, ‘So-and-so, so-and-so has vowed an offering to you and will bring it tomorrow. I have fulfilled his need for your sake.’” The following morning, that person would bring him the vowed offering, and this unbelieving shaykh would disclose hidden matters to him.

He said: “Whenever I was asked to perform a transformation involving something such as labdanum, I would say, ‘Wait until I lose awareness.’ Suddenly, the labdanum would be in my hand or my mouth, without my knowing who had put it there.” He also said: “I used to walk with a black column before me, with a light upon it.”

When this shaykh repented and began praying, fasting, and avoiding forbidden acts, the black dog disappeared, and the transformations ceased: neither labdanum nor anything else was brought to him.

Another shaykh had devils whom he would send to possess certain people, and the family of that person would come

---

## Page 16557

to the shaykh, asking him to cure the possessed person. He would send word to his followers, who would leave that person, and the family would pay the shaykh large sums of money. Sometimes the jinn would bring him money and food stolen from people. One person, for instance, had figs in a storage bin. The shaykh asked his devils for figs, and they brought them to him. When the owners of the bin went looking for their figs, they found them gone.

Another man was engaged in learning and recitation when devils came and tempted him, saying: “We shall exempt you from prayer and bring you whatever you want.” They would bring him sweets and fruit, until he visited a shaykh knowledgeable about the Sunnah. The shaykh called upon him to repent and paid the owners of the sweets for what that man, deluded by the devil, had eaten.

Thus, anyone who departs from the Quran and Sunnah and experiences an extraordinary state—whether involving disclosure of hidden matters or the power to produce effects—has a state arising either from his own soul or from devils. If he has no such state but merely imitates those who do, his state is a fabrication.

Those with satanic states generally combine the satanic state with the fabricated one, as Allah Most High says:

“Shall I inform you upon whom the devils descend?”

“They descend upon every sinful liar.”

Al-Hallāj was one of the leaders of these people: those with satanic and fabricated states. These people comprise many groups.

---

## Page 16558

The leaders of these people are the religious leaders of the polytheists who worship idols, such as the soothsayers and sorcerers among the polytheist Arabs, and the soothsayers in India, among the Turks, and elsewhere. Some of these people believe that when someone among them dies, he returns after death, speaks to them, settles his debts, returns what was entrusted to him, and gives them instructions. The figure they knew during his lifetime does indeed appear to them, but it is a devil assuming his likeness, whom they mistake for him.

Many who call upon shaykhs for help say:

“O my master so-and-so!” or “O Shaykh so-and-so, fulfil my need!”

Then they see a figure resembling that shaykh addressing them and saying:

“I shall fulfil your need and put your heart at ease.”

It then fulfils the person’s need or wards off his enemy. Yet it is a devil that has assumed the shaykh’s likeness, after that person associated partners with Allah by calling upon someone other than Him. I know of numerous incidents of this kind. Indeed, a number of my companions mentioned that they had called upon me for help during hardships that befell them.

One feared the Armenians, and another feared the Tatars. Each related that when he called upon me for help, he saw me in the air, warding off his enemy. I informed them that I had been unaware of this and had warded off nothing from them. Rather, it was a devil that had appeared to one of them and led him astray when he associated partners with Allah Most High.

The same has happened to several shaykhs among our companions in their dealings with their followers: one of them calls upon the shaykh for help and sees

---

## Page 16559

the shaykh come and fulfil his need. Yet that shaykh says, “I knew nothing of this.” It thus becomes clear that it was a devil.

One of our companions told me that he had called for help upon two men in whom he had faith. They had come to him in the air and said, “Set your heart at ease. We shall ward these people off from you and do such-and-such.” I asked him, “Did any of that happen?” He replied, “No.” This was among the things that showed him that they were two devils. For although devils may tell someone something true about an incident or an account, their lies far outnumber their truths, just as the jinn used to give information to soothsayers. Thus, whoever relies upon his disclosures of hidden matters when these come from information supplied by the jinn will speak falsely more often than truthfully.

One example was a shaykh called al-Shayyāh, whom we brought to repentance and had renew his Islam. He had a companion from among the jinn called ʿAntar, who told him things, sometimes truthfully and sometimes falsely. When I told him, “You are worshipping a devil instead of Allah,” he admitted that he would say to him, “O ʿAntar, no glory be to you! You are a filthy god.” He repented of this in a well-known incident.

The sword of the Sacred Law has put a number of these people to death, including the man whom we put to death in the year fifteen. He had a companion who would come to him and disclose hidden matters to him, sometimes truthfully and sometimes falsely. A group of people associated with learning and leadership had submitted to him, and he would disclose hidden matters to them, until Allah exposed him to them.

This happened because his companion would sometimes say to him, “I am the Messenger of Allah,” and mention things

---

## Page 16560

incompatible with the Messenger’s character. Testimony was given against him that he had said, “The Messenger comes to me and tells me such-and-such”—things which it is disbelief to attribute to the Messenger. I explained to those in authority that this man was a soothsayer of sorts and that what he saw was a devil. That is why it did not come to him in the Prophet’s known appearance, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, but in an unfamiliar form. He would report that this figure humbled itself before him and permitted him to consume intoxicants and do other things. Many people thought he was lying about what he had seen. He was not lying about having seen that form, but he was a disbeliever for believing it to be the Messenger of Allah. Such cases are numerous.

Thus, devils descend upon them according to how far they have fulfilled Satan’s wishes. The further they stray from Allah, His Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and the path of the believers, the closer they come to Satan. They fly through the air, but it is Satan who carries them. Some strike down those present, but it is their devils that have struck them down. Some produce food and accompaniments and fill a jug with water from the air, but it is the devils that have done this. The ignorant imagine these to be miraculous gifts granted to Allah’s pious allies, whereas they are merely manifestations like those experienced by sorcerers, soothsayers and their like.

Whoever fails to distinguish between states that come from the Most Merciful and those arising from the self will confuse truth with falsehood. Whoever’s heart Allah has not illuminated with the realities of faith and adherence to the Quran will not distinguish the path of one who follows the truth from

---

## Page 16561

that of one who follows falsehood; the matter and the true state of affairs will become confused for him, just as people were confused about Musaylima of al-Yamāma and other liars who claimed to be prophets, although they were nothing but liars. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“The Hour will not come until there are thirty lying impostors among you, each claiming to be the Messenger of Allah.”

The impostor who brings the greatest trial is “the great Dajjāl,” whom Jesus son of Mary will kill. From the time of Adam until the coming of the Hour, Allah has created no trial greater than his. The Prophet instructed Muslims to seek refuge from his trial in their prayers. It is established that “he will say to the sky, ‘Rain!’ and it will rain, and to the earth, ‘Bring forth vegetation!’ and it will bring forth vegetation,” and that “he will kill a believing man, then say to him, ‘Rise!’ and he will rise. He will say, ‘I am your Lord,’ but the man will reply, ‘You lie! Rather, you are the one-eyed liar about whom the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, told us. By Allah, I have only become more certain about you.’ Thus he kills him twice, then seeks to kill him a third time, but Allah does not give him power over him.” He claims divinity.

The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, made clear three signs concerning him that contradict his claim:

The first is that “he is one-eyed, whereas your Lord is not one-eyed.”

The second is that “between his eyes is written kāfir (disbeliever), which every believer will read, whether literate or illiterate.”

The third is his saying:

“Know that none of you will see his Lord until he dies.”

---

## Page 16562

This, then, is the great Dajjāl. There are lesser impostors: some claim prophethood, while others are deemed liars without claiming prophethood. As the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“At the end of time there will be lying impostors who tell you things that neither you nor your forefathers have heard. So beware of them.”

Al-Hallāj was undoubtedly one of these impostors. But if asked, “Did he repent before death or not?” one should say, “Allah knows best,” and not speak without knowledge. Nevertheless, the statements and deeds that became known about him established his disbelief and warranted his execution, by the consensus of the Muslims. Allah knows best about him.

---

## Page 16563

He—may Allah Most High have mercy on him—was asked about “al-Mu‘izz Ma‘add ibn Tamīm,” who built Cairo and the two palaces:

Was he of noble descent through Fātimah? Were he and his descendants infallible, and did they possess esoteric knowledge? If they were not of noble descent, what evidence supports that position? If they acted contrary to the Sharia, were they “rebels” or not? What is the ruling concerning the reliable scholars, whose statements are accepted as evidence, who reported this about them? Please explain this matter at length.

He replied:

Praise be to Allah. As for the claim that he, any of his descendants, or others like them were infallible—free from sin and error—as the Rāfidah claim concerning the “Twelve,” this claim is far worse than that of the Rāfidah. For the Rāfidah made this claim concerning people whose faith and piety are beyond doubt—indeed, people who are undoubtedly among the inhabitants of Paradise, such as ‘Alī, al-Hasan and al-Husayn, may Allah be pleased with them.

Even so, the people of knowledge and faith agree that this is among the most corrupt of claims, and that it is a claim made by purveyors of lies and slander. Infallibility in these matters belongs to none but the prophets, peace be upon them. The statements of anyone other than the prophets may be accepted or rejected. Obedience to anyone other than the prophets and messengers is not obligatory in everything he says, nor are people obliged to follow him and believe

---

## Page 16564

him in everything he commands or reports; nor does opposing him in these matters constitute disbelief, unlike the case with the prophets.

Rather, if one of his peers disagrees with him, a scholar qualified to exercise independent judgment must examine both positions and follow whichever is more closely aligned with the Quran and Sunnah. As Allah Most High says:

“O you who believe, obey Allah and obey the Messenger and those in authority among you. If you disagree over anything, refer it to Allah and the Messenger, if you believe in Allah and the Last Day. That is better and fairest in outcome.”

He thus commands that disputes be referred to Allah and the Messenger, for one who is infallible speaks only the truth. Anyone known to have spoken the truth on a disputed matter must be followed—for example, when he cites a verse from the Book of Allah Most High or an authentic hadith from the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, intending thereby to settle the dispute. But the claim that someone must be followed in everything he says, without his presenting evidence establishing its correctness, is not valid. Rather, this is “the rank of the Messenger,” which belongs to him alone. As Allah Most High says:

“But no, by your Lord, they will not believe until they make you judge in their disputes, then find within themselves no discomfort over your judgment and submit wholeheartedly.”

Allah Most High also says:

“We did not send any messenger except to be obeyed by Allah’s permission. If, when they wronged themselves, they had come to you and sought Allah’s forgiveness, and the Messenger had sought forgiveness for them, they would have found Allah ever accepting of repentance, Most Merciful.”

Allah Most High also says:

“Say: If you love Allah, then follow me; Allah will love you.”

Allah Most High also says:

“It is not for a believing man or woman, when Allah and His Messenger have decided a matter, to have any choice in their affair.”

Allah Most High also says:

“The only response of the believers, when they are called to Allah and His Messenger

---

## Page 16565

to judge between them, is to say, ‘We hear and obey.’ It is they who are successful.”

He also says:

“Whoever obeys Allah and the Messenger will be with those whom Allah has blessed: the prophets, the steadfastly truthful, the martyrs, and the righteous. What excellent companions they are!”

Allah Most High also says:

“These are the limits set by Allah. Whoever obeys Allah and His Messenger, He will admit them into Gardens beneath which rivers flow, to remain there forever. That is the great triumph.”

“But whoever disobeys Allah and His Messenger and transgresses His limits, He will admit him into a Fire, to remain there forever, and he will have a humiliating punishment.”

Allah Most High also says:

“Messengers bearing good news and warnings, so that people would have no argument against Allah after the messengers.”

Allah Most High also says:

“We never punish until We have sent a messenger.”

Allah Most High also says:

“If you establish prayer, give zakah, believe in My messengers and support them, and lend Allah a good loan, I will surely absolve you of your misdeeds.”

There are many such passages in the Quran in which He explains the bliss of those who believe in the messengers, follow them, and obey them, and the misery of those who do not believe in them or follow them, but instead disobey them. If anyone other than the Messenger were infallible in what he commands and forbids, his standing in this respect would be the same as that of the Messenger. A prophet sent to people is a messenger to them, unlike one who has not been sent to them.

Thus, if anyone who issues commands and prohibitions to people—whether an imam, a scholar, a religious guide, or any other person in authority, from the Prophet’s household or otherwise—were infallible, he would have the same standing as the Messenger in this respect. Whoever obeyed him would be assured of Paradise, and whoever disobeyed him would be condemned to the Fire, as those who assert the infallibility of ‘Alī or other imams maintain. Indeed, whoever obeyed him would be a believer, and whoever disobeyed him would be a disbeliever. These people would be like the prophets of the Children of Israel, and it would therefore not

---

## Page 16566

be valid, in that case, for the Prophet (may Allah bless him and grant him peace) to say, “There is no prophet after me.”

It is also reported from him in the Sunan collections that he said:

“The scholars are the heirs of the prophets. The prophets left neither a dirham nor a dinar as an inheritance; they left only knowledge. Whoever acquires it has acquired an abundant share.”

Thus, the highest standing that scholars of this nation—whether imams or others—can attain is to be heirs of prophets.

Moreover, it is established by authentic texts and consensus that the Prophet (may Allah bless him and grant him peace) said to al-Siddīq concerning a dream he had interpreted, “You were right in some respects and mistaken in others.” Al-Siddīq also said, “Obey me as long as I obey Allah. If I disobey Allah, you owe me no obedience.”

Once, when he became angry with a man, Abū Burdah said to him, “Let me strike off his head.” He asked, “Would you have done so?” He replied, “Yes.” He then said, “No one after the Messenger of Allah (may Allah bless him and grant him peace) has that right.”

For this reason, the imams agreed that whoever insults a prophet is to be executed, whereas someone who insults anyone other than a prophet is not to be executed for just any insult; rather, distinctions must be made. Someone who accuses the Prophet’s mother of unchastity is to be executed, whether Muslim or disbeliever, because he has impugned the Prophet’s lineage. But if he accuses a woman other than the Prophet’s mother (may Allah bless him and grant him peace), whose innocence is not known, he is not to be executed.

Similarly, ‘Umar ibn al-Khattāb acknowledged such fallibility in himself on various occasions. He would retract many statements when it became clear to him that the truth was contrary to what he had said. He would ask the Companions about aspects of the Sunnah so that he could learn them from them. On various occasions he would say, “By Allah, ‘Umar does not know whether he has reached the truth or missed it.” He would also say, “A woman was right, and a man was mistaken.” Nevertheless, it is established in

---

## Page 16567

the two Sahīh collections that the Prophet (may Allah bless him and grant him peace) said:

“Among the nations before you were men granted inspiration. If there is anyone such in my nation, it is ‘Umar.”

In al-Tirmidhī, it is reported:

“Had I not been sent among you, ‘Umar would have been sent among you.”

He also said:

“Allah has placed the truth upon ‘Umar’s tongue and heart.”

If this man granted inspiration, upon whose tongue and heart Allah placed the truth, acknowledges despite his standing that he is not infallible, what, then, of the other Companions and those besides them who have not attained his standing? The scholars agree that Abū Bakr and ‘Umar were more knowledgeable than all the other Companions, more obedient to Allah and His Messenger, and better able to recognize and follow the truth. It has been established through authentic, mass-transmitted reports that the Prophet (may Allah bless him and grant him peace) said:

“The best of this nation after its Prophet is Abū Bakr, then ‘Umar.”

This has been narrated from him through some eighty routes of transmission. ‘Alī (may Allah be pleased with him) said, “If anyone is brought before me who regards me as superior to Abū Bakr and ‘Umar, I will have him flogged with the prescribed punishment for slander.” Numerous statements have been transmitted from ‘Uthmān, ‘Alī, and other Companions.

Indeed, no legal ruling is recorded from Abū Bakr al-Siddīq in which he ruled contrary to an explicit statement of the Prophet (may Allah bless him and grant him peace). More instances of this have been found in the rulings of ‘Alī and other Companions than in those of ‘Umar. Al-Shāfi‘ī (may Allah be pleased with him) used to debate certain jurists of Kufa on questions of jurisprudence, and they would cite ‘Alī’s opinions against him. He therefore compiled a book entitled “The Disagreements of ‘Alī and ‘Abd Allāh ibn Mas‘ūd,” in which he explained numerous rulings of theirs that were set aside because the Sunnah established otherwise. After him, Muhammad ibn Nasr al-Marwazī compiled an even larger book on the subject. For example, among the opinions of ‘Alī (may Allah be pleased with him) that were set aside was his view

---

## Page 16568

that a pregnant widow must observe her waiting period until the later of the two prescribed terms. This view is also reported from Ibn ‘Abbās. The leading authorities in legal verdicts, however, agreed with ‘Uthmān, Ibn Mas‘ūd, and others that she becomes free to marry upon giving birth. This is based on the established report concerning the Prophet (may Allah bless him and grant him peace):

“Subay‘a al-Aslamiyya gave birth a few nights after her husband’s death. Abū al-Sanābil ibn Ba‘kak visited her and said, ‘You may not marry until four months and ten days have passed.’ She therefore asked the Prophet (may Allah bless him and grant him peace) about this, and he said, ‘Abū al-Sanābil has spoken falsely. You are free to marry, so marry.’”

Thus, the Prophet (may Allah bless him and grant him peace) declared that whoever gave this verdict had spoken falsely.

Likewise, concerning a woman whose husband married her without specifying a dower and then died without assigning her one, ‘Alī and Ibn ‘Abbās held that she was not entitled to a dower. Ibn Mas‘ūd and others ruled that she was entitled to the customary dower of a woman of comparable standing. A man from Ashja‘ then stood up and said:

“We testify that the Messenger of Allah (may Allah bless him and grant him peace) ruled concerning Birwa‘ bint Wāshiq just as you have ruled concerning this woman.”

There are many such examples. ‘Alī, his two sons, and others differed among themselves in scholarly matters and legal verdicts, just as other scholars differ among themselves. Had they been infallible, it would have been impossible for one infallible person to disagree with another. Al-Hasan disagreed with his father over fighting and disapproved of much of what he did. ‘Alī (may Allah be pleased with him) would ultimately come round to his son’s view, and he used to say:

“If I have faltered once, I make no excuse;
Henceforth I shall act wisely and persevere,
And mend my scattered, nasīb judgment.”

---

## Page 16569

and toward the end of his life it became clear to him that acting otherwise than he had would have been more correct. He also issued legal verdicts in which he reversed earlier rulings. For example, he held two views concerning slave women who had borne their masters’ children: one prohibited selling them, while the other permitted it. An infallible person cannot hold two contradictory positions unless one abrogates the other, as could occur with the statements of the Prophet (may Allah bless him and grant him peace). The Sunnah, however, has been definitively established and cannot undergo abrogation after him, since there is no prophet after him.

Al-Hasan counselled his brother al-Husayn not to obey the people of Iraq or seek leadership. Ibn ‘Umar, Ibn ‘Abbās, and others who were devoted to him and loved him gave him the same advice. They considered it in his interests and those of the Muslims that he should not go to them or accept their invitation to come and fight alongside them. Even if this was what served his interests and those of the Muslims, he (may Allah be pleased with him) nevertheless acted according to what he considered beneficial. Human judgment may be right or wrong.

No one may oppose an infallible person, nor may an infallible person oppose another infallible person, unless they follow two different religious laws, as two messengers might. Yet it is well known that their religious law was one and the same. This is an extensive subject, discussed at length elsewhere.

The point is that anyone who claims infallibility for these noble figures, whose faith, piety, and admission to Paradise are attested, is utterly astray and ignorant. No one with an honourable reputation within the Muslim community has voiced this claim—not even anyone possessed of sound judgment.

---

## Page 16570

How, then, could the descendants of “ʿUbayd Allāh ibn Maymūn al-Qaddāh” be infallible, given their notorious hypocrisy, lying, and misguidance? Even supposing this were not so, there is no doubt that their conduct is that of kings—and among the worst in oppression and the violation of sacred prohibitions, the furthest from putting affairs in order and fulfilling obligations, and the most flagrant in promoting innovations contrary to the Book and the Sunnah and aiding the people of hypocrisy and innovation.

The scholars have agreed that the Umayyad and Abbasid dynasties were closer to Allah and His Messenger than their dynasty, possessed greater knowledge and faith, and were less given to innovation and wickedness. They also agree that the caliphs of those two dynasties were more obedient to Allah and His Messenger than the caliphs of their dynasty. Yet none of the caliphs of those two dynasties could legitimately be described as infallible. How, then, can someone claim infallibility when his obscenities, reprehensible deeds, oppression, tyranny, aggression, hostility toward the righteous and pious members of the Muslim community, and trust in the people of disbelief and hypocrisy have become manifest? They are among the most wicked of people and the most deeply entrenched in disbelief. Only someone steeped in ignorance, or a heretic speaking without knowledge, would claim infallibility in the face of such hypocrisy and wickedness.

It is known beyond doubt that anyone who attests to their faith and piety, or to the authenticity of their lineage, has testified to something of which he has no knowledge. Allah Most High has said:

“Do not pursue that of which you have no knowledge.”

And He Most High has said:

“Except those who testify to the truth knowingly.”

And He said concerning Joseph’s brothers:

“We testified only to what we knew.”

No one knows the authenticity of their lineage

---

## Page 16571

or the genuineness of their faith and piety; for the most anyone can claim is that they outwardly professed Islam and adherence to its laws. Not everyone who outwardly professes Islam is inwardly a believer, for those who profess Islam are known to include both believers and hypocrites. Allah Most High has said:

“Among the people are some who say, ‘We believe in Allah and the Last Day,’ yet they are not believers.”

And He Most High has said:

“When the hypocrites come to you, they say, ‘We bear witness that you are indeed the Messenger of Allah.’ Allah knows that you are indeed His Messenger, and Allah bears witness that the hypocrites are liars.”

And He Most High has said:

“The Bedouins say, ‘We believe.’ Say, ‘You have not believed; rather, say, “We have submitted,” for faith has not yet entered your hearts.’”

The scholars, leading authorities, and overwhelming majority of the Muslim community testify that these people were hypocrites and heretics who outwardly professed Islam while inwardly concealing disbelief. Even supposing that some people disagreed with them about this, their faith would then be a matter of well-known dispute. Anyone who attests to their faith is therefore testifying to something of which he has no knowledge, for he has no evidence of their faith comparable to the evidence his opponents possess of their hypocrisy and heresy.

The same applies to their “lineage.” It is well known that the majority of the Muslim community impugn their lineage and state that they are descendants of Magians or Jews. This is widely attested by scholars of the various groups—Hanafīs, Mālikīs, Shāfiʿīs, Hanbalīs, hadith scholars, scholastic theologians, and genealogists—as well as by the common people and others. This matter has been mentioned by most writers who record people’s histories and the events of their times, including even some who might reserve judgment about them, such as Ibn al-Athīr al-Mawsilī in his history and others like him. He recorded what Muslim scholars had written in their own hands impugning their lineage.

---

## Page 16572

As for the majority of earlier and later authors—including even Judge Ibn Khallikān in his history—they declared their claimed lineage invalid. So too did Ibn al-Jawzī, Abū Shāmah, and other scholars knowledgeable about the matter. Indeed, scholars wrote works exposing their secrets and stripping away their concealment. Judge Abū Bakr al-Bāqillānī, for example, wrote his well-known book exposing their secrets and stripping away their concealment. He stated that they were descendants of Magians and described aspects of their doctrines, demonstrating that these were worse than the doctrines of the Jews and Christians, and even those of the extremists who claim that Ali is divine or a prophet. Their disbelief is thus even greater than that of these extremists.

Likewise, Judge Abū Yaʿlā included in his book al-Muʿtamad (The Authoritative Work) a lengthy section explaining their heresy and disbelief. Abū Hāmid al-Ghazālī likewise discussed them in the book he entitled Fadā’il al-Mustazhiriyyah wa-Fadā’ih al-Bātiniyyah (The Merits of the Mustazhiriyyah and the Scandals of the Bātiniyyah). He said: “Outwardly, their doctrine is Rāfidī Shiism; inwardly, it is pure disbelief.”

Similarly, Judge ʿAbd al-Jabbār ibn Ahmad and others like him among the Muʿtazilīs with Shīʿī leanings—who regard no one as superior to Ali, and indeed declare those who fought him and did not repent of doing so to be wicked—count these people among the foremost hypocrites and heretics. Such is the Muʿtazilīs’ judgment of them; what, then, must be the judgment of Ahl al-Sunnah wa-l-Jamāʿah?

Even the Imāmī Rāfidīs—although they are among the most ignorant of people and possess neither reason nor transmitted authority, neither a sound religion nor worldly ascendancy—do indeed know that these people’s doctrine is that of hypocritical heretics; and they know that the doctrine of these people

---

## Page 16573

—the Bātiniyyah—is worse than that of the extremists who believe in the divinity of Ali, may Allah be pleased with him. As for the challenges to their lineage, these have been reported from the great majority of the Muslim community’s scholars across its various groups. Other groups besides them held the caliphate, and some of them were guilty of innovation and oppression. Yet people did not challenge any of their lineages as they challenged these people’s lineage, nor accuse them of heresy and hypocrisy as they accused these people.

Various groups among Ali’s descendants rose up, from both the descendants of al-Hasan and those of al-Husayn, including Muhammad ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Hasan, his brother Ibrāhīm ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Hasan, and others like them. No one, whether an enemy or otherwise, questioned either their lineage or their Islam. The same applies to al-Dāʿī, who established himself in Tabaristān, and other descendants of Ali, as well as the Banū Hammūd, who seized power in al-Andalus for a time. No one challenged the lineage or Islam of these people and others like them.

A number of the descendants of Abū Tālib were killed in struggles over the caliphate, especially under Abbasid rule, while others, such as Mūsā ibn Jaʿfar, were imprisoned. Yet their enemies did not challenge their lineage or religion.

The reason is that well-known lineages are plain and repeatedly attested, like the sun, whose light no enemy can extinguish. Likewise, a man’s Islam and the soundness of his faith in Allah and the Messenger are not hidden matters. When someone’s lineage and religion are so widely known, an enemy could not discredit them even if he wished to do so. Such matters are transmitted by many people with ample interest and incentive to report them. Nor could the scholars unanimously agree on such a denial.

---

## Page 16574

As for these “Banū ʿUbayd al-Qaddāh,” the community’s scholars whose knowledge and religious integrity are trusted have always challenged their lineage and religion. They do not condemn them simply for Rāfidism and Shiism, for many others share those views. Rather, they class them among “the Bātinī Qarmatians,” who include the Ismāʿīliyyah and Nusayriyyah. Of the same kind are the Khurramiyyah-Muhammirah and similar disbelieving hypocrites who outwardly professed Islam while concealing disbelief.

There is no doubt that following these people is false. Scholars have described the leaders of this doctrine as the very people who invented and fabricated it. They have explained the foundations of their doctrines and how they borrowed some teachings from the Magians and others from the philosophers. Thus they devised such ranks as “the Preceder,” “the Follower,” “the Foundation,” “the Proofs,” and “the Claims.” Their call is organized into seven degrees, the last being “al-balāgh al-akbar (the greatest disclosure) and al-nāmūs al-aʿzam (the supreme law).” This is not the place to explain these matters in detail.

That being so, anyone who testifies to the authenticity of their lineage or their faith is, at the very least, testifying without knowledge and asserting something of which he has no knowledge. This is forbidden by the consensus of the Muslim community. Indeed, their manifest heresy, hypocrisy, and hostility towards what the Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, brought are evidence that their claimed descent from Fātimah is false. For a relative of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, who holds the caliphate over his community would not oppose his religion as these people do.

No caliph among the Banū Hāshim, the descendants of Abū Tālib, or the Banū Umayyah has been known to be hostile towards the religion of Islam, let alone to show hostility like that of

---

## Page 16575

these people. Indeed, even the children of kings who have no religion retain some protective zeal for the religion of their fathers and forebears. How, then, could a descendant of the master of Adam’s children, whom Allah sent with guidance and the religion of truth, show such hostility towards his religion? Thus, we find all those who can be trusted to uphold Islam, inwardly and outwardly, opposed to these people. The only exceptions are heretics who are enemies of Allah and His Messenger, or ignorant people who do not know what His Messenger was sent with. This is further evidence of their disbelief and the falsity of their claimed lineage.

Section

As for the questioner’s statement that “they possess esoteric knowledge,” their very claim to such knowledge is the strongest proof and evidence that they are heretical hypocrites who believe neither in Allah, nor in His Messenger, nor in the Last Day. For this esoteric knowledge they claim is disbelief by the agreement of Muslims, Jews, and Christians; indeed, most polytheists also regard it as disbelief. Its substance is the claim that the divine scriptures have hidden meanings that contradict what believers know concerning commands, prohibitions, and revealed accounts.

As for “commands,” people know beyond doubt, as part of the religion of Islam, that Muhammad, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, commanded them to perform the prescribed prayers, pay the obligatory zakah, fast the month of Ramadan, and perform pilgrimage to the Ancient House.

---

## Page 16576

As for “prohibitions,” Allah Most High has forbidden them shameful deeds, whether open or secret, sin, wrongful aggression, associating with Allah anything for which He has sent down no authority, and saying about Allah what they do not know. He has likewise forbidden wine, marriage to women within the prohibited degrees of kinship, usury, gambling, and other such things.

These people claim that such injunctions do not mean what Muslims understand them to mean. Rather, they have hidden meanings known to these Ismā‘īlī imams who trace their descent to Muhammad ibn Ismā‘īl ibn Ja‘far, whom they claim to be infallible and possessors of esoteric knowledge. For example, they say: “Prayer” means knowing our secrets, not these prayers involving bowing, prostration, and recitation. “Fasting” means concealing our secrets, not abstaining from food, drink, and sexual intercourse. “Pilgrimage” means visiting our holy shaykhs, and so forth.

Those who lay claim to hidden meanings do not regard these acts of worship as obligatory or these forbidden things as unlawful. Rather, they deem shameful deeds lawful, whether open or secret, as well as marriage to mothers and daughters and other abominations. It is well known that their disbelief is greater than that of Jews and Christians. How, then, can anyone like this be infallible?

As for “revealed accounts,” they do not acknowledge that people will rise from their graves before the Lord of all worlds, nor the reward and punishment Allah has promised His servants. They do not even acknowledge what the Messengers reported concerning the angels, or indeed what they related concerning Allah’s names and attributes. Rather, in the accounts they accept, they follow the Peripatetic philosophers, the followers of Aristotle, and they seek to reconcile

---

## Page 16577

what the Messengers reported with what these men say, as did the authors of the “Epistles of the Brethren of Purity.” They follow the path of these ‘Ubaydids, the descendants of “‘Ubayd Allah ibn Maymūn al-Qaddāh.”

Can anyone acquainted with the religion of the Muslims, Jews, or Christians deny that the teachings of the authors of the “Epistles of the Brethren of Purity” contradict all three religions, even though these writings contain knowledge of undeniable merit in mathematics, the natural sciences, some branches of logic and metaphysics, and the disciplines of ethics, politics, and household management? For they contradict what the Messengers reported and commanded, deny much of what they brought, and alter the revealed laws of all the Messengers in ways plain to anyone acquainted with any of these religions. These people therefore stand outside all three religions.

Among their lies and false claims is that these “Epistles” are the words of Ja‘far ibn Muhammad al-Sādiq. Scholars know that they were only composed after the third century AH, around the time Cairo was built. Their author mentions events that befell the Muslim world, such as the Christians’ seizure of the Syrian coastal regions and other events that occurred after the third century AH. Ja‘far ibn Muhammad—may Allah be pleased with him—died in 148 AH, more than two hundred years before Cairo was built, for Cairo was built around 360 AH, as stated in the “History of al-Azhar Mosque.” It is said that its construction began in 358 AH and that in 362 AH, “Ma‘d ibn Tamīm” arrived from the Maghrib and settled there.

---

## Page 16578

This is further illustrated by the fact that the philosophers known to have departed from the religion of Islam were followers of Mubashshir ibn Fātik, one of their emirs, and Abū ‘Alī ibn al-Haytham. Both lived near al-Azhar Mosque under those rulers. Ibn Sīnā, his son, and his brother were followers of these two.

Ibn Sīnā said:

“I studied some philosophy, and I used to hear my father and brother discussing ‘the intellect’ and ‘the soul.’”

He lived during the reign of al-Hākim. Al-Hākim’s conduct is well known, as is what Hashkīn al-Durzī did at his command: calling people to worship al-Hākim, fighting the people of Egypt over this, and then going to Syria, where he eventually led the people of Wādī al-Taym ibn Tha‘laba astray. Heresy and hypocrisy persist among them to this day. They possess the books of al-Hākim; I obtained these from them and read what they contain concerning their worship of al-Hākim, his releasing them from the obligations of prayer, zakat, fasting, and pilgrimage, and their branding as Hashwiyya those Muslims who affirm these obligations and prohibit what Allah and His Messenger have forbidden. They contain countless other forms of hypocrisy of this kind.
