In short, the “esoteric knowledge” they claim consists of disbelief in Allah, His angels, His books, His Messengers, and the Last Day. Indeed, it encompasses every form of disbelief. Yet they differ in degree: they are not all equal in disbelief. They divide this knowledge into seven levels, addressing a particular group of people at each level according to their distance from or closeness to religion.

They have titles and hierarchies assembled from the doctrines of the Magians, the philosophers, and the Rāfida, such as their expressions “the Preceding One” and “the Following One,” which they make correspond to “the intellect”

---

## Page 16579

and “the soul,” as discussed by the philosophers, and to light and darkness, as discussed by the Magians. They profess allegiance to Muhammad ibn Ismā‘īl ibn Ja‘far and claim that he is the seventh. They speak of esoteric knowledge, “the Foundation,” “the Proof,” “the Gate,” and other matters too lengthy to describe.

Among their instructions in al-Nāmūs al-Akbar wa-l-Balāgh al-A‘zam is that they should approach Muslims through “the gateway of Shiism.” This is because they know that the Shiites are among the most ignorant groups, the weakest in reason and knowledge, and the furthest from the religion of Islam in knowledge and practice. Thus, both in earlier times and more recently, heretics have infiltrated Islam through those professing Shiism, just as warring unbelievers entered the cities of Islam, including Baghdad, with Shiite assistance. This happened under the rule of the unbelieving Turks in Baghdad, Aleppo, and elsewhere; indeed, similar things occurred through changes among the Muslims in their relations with Christians and others.

They present themselves as Shiites to those they seek to recruit. When someone responds to them, they lead him into Rāfidism and disparagement of the Companions. If they find him receptive, they lead him into attacking ‘Alī and others. Then they lead him into disparaging our Prophet and all the other prophets, saying:

“The prophets have hidden teachings and secrets contrary to what their communities follow. They were intelligent, accomplished men who advanced their worldly aims through the religious codes they devised.”

Then they disparage the Messiah, claiming that Joseph the carpenter was his father. They portray him as lacking sound judgment, on the grounds that his enemy gained power over him and crucified him. Thus they agree with the Jews in disparaging the Messiah. Yet they are worse than the Jews, for they disparage the prophets. As for Moses and Muhammad, however, they exalt their standing because of their power and their subjugation of

---

## Page 16580

their enemy. They claim that whatever scripture those two made public was intended to keep the common people at bay, and that it contains hidden secrets: whoever knows them becomes one of those who have attained complete perfection.

They say, “Allah has made lawful for us whatever we desire of obscenities and evil deeds, and the taking of people’s wealth by any means. None of the duties incumbent upon the common people—prayer, zakat, fasting, and so forth—is incumbent upon us.” For, in their view, someone who has attained this level knows that there is neither Paradise nor Hell, neither reward nor punishment.

Their leaders hold two positions regarding the affirmation of “the Necessary Existent,” the Originator of the world. Some deny Him and claim that the Peripatetic philosophers disagree on everything except the Necessary Existent. They treat the remembrance of Allah and His name with contempt, to the point that one of them writes the name of Allah and the name of His Messenger on his nether parts. There are many similar examples of their unbelief.

The proponent of the well-known missionary call, together with the Ismā‘īlīs who followed this doctrine in the fortresses of Alamūt and elsewhere in Khurāsān, in Yemen, in the mountains of Syria, and elsewhere, adhered to the doctrine of the ‘Ubaydids about whom the question was asked.

Ibn al-Sabbāh, who headed the Ismā‘īlīs and whose followers al-Ghazālī used to debate, came to Egypt during the reign of al-Mustansir—whose reign was the longest among them—and learned their secrets from him. During al-Mustansir’s reign, al-Basāsīrī’s uprising occurred in the fifth century, in the year 450 AH, when al-Basāsīrī took up arms in rebellion against the Abbasid caliph al-Qā’im bi-Amr Allāh

---

## Page 16581

and allied himself with the ‘Ubaydid al-Mustansir and began mustering troops for Iraq. They instituted the distinctive practices of the Rāfidah in Syria and Iraq, as they had already done in Egypt, and killed groups of Muslim scholars and shaykhs, just as their predecessors had previously killed groups of them in the Maghreb. From the pulpits they proclaimed the call:

“Come to the best of deeds.”

This continued until the Seljuk Turks, who were rulers of the Muslims, came, defeated them, and drove them back to Egypt. Among the last of these rulers was “the martyr Nūr al-Dīn Mahmūd,” who conquered most of Syria and rescued it from Christian hands. He then sent his army to Egypt when its people appealed to him for help against the Franks. The army entered Egypt repeatedly with Saladin, who conquered it, ended the missionary call of the ‘Ubaydids—the esotericist Qarmatians—and established the laws of Islam there. From that time onward, people who openly professed Islam came to live there.

During their rule, anyone living in Egypt feared being killed for narrating a hadith from the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. This was related by Ibrāhīm ibn Sa‘d al-Habbāl, the associate of ‘Abd al-Ghanī ibn Sa‘īd, who refrained from narrating hadith for fear that they would kill him. They used to proclaim between the two palaces:

“Whoever curses and reviles will receive a dinar and an irdabb (a measure of grain).”

The al-Azhar Mosque contained several enclosed sections where the Companions were cursed; indeed, statements of outright unbelief were uttered there. They also had a school near the “shrine” they had built and attributed to al-Husayn, although, by scholarly agreement, neither al-Husayn nor any part of his remains is there. In their school they did not teach the Islamic sciences, but rather logic, natural philosophy, metaphysics, and similar doctrines of the philosophers. They built observatories on

---

## Page 16582

mountains and elsewhere, where they observed the stars, worshipped and glorified them, and summoned their spirits to descend. These are devils that descend upon disbelieving polytheists, like the devils associated with idols and the like.

“Al-Mu‘izz ibn Tamīm ibn Ma‘add,” the first of them to enter Cairo, composed a work on this subject that is well known among his followers. This is not “al-Mu‘izz ibn Bādīs,” for the latter was a Muslim who followed the Sunnah and was one of the rulers of the Maghreb. The former came some time after him.

Because of their heretical unbelief and religious innovation, the light of Islam and faith remained extinguished throughout Egypt during their rule, which lasted some two hundred years. Scholars even described it as a land of apostasy and hypocrisy, like the land of Musaylimah the Liar and of “the Qarmatians” who rose up in Iraq and were the predecessors of these Qarmatians. They went from Iraq to the Maghreb, then came from the Maghreb to Egypt. Their unbelief and apostasy are among the gravest forms of unbelief and apostasy, exceeding even those of the followers of Musaylimah the Liar and other such liars. Those people did not make the claims concerning divinity, lordship, and religious laws that the leaders of these people made.

For this reason, their graves are distinguished from Muslim graves, just as Muslim graves are distinguished from those of unbelievers: their graves face away from the qiblah. When horses suffer from colic, people bring them to their graves, just as they bring them to the graves of unbelievers. This is a well-known practice concerning horses: when horses suffer from colic, people take them to the graves of

---

## Page 16583

Christians in Damascus. If they are in areas inhabited by the Ismāʿīlīs, Nusayrīs, or similar groups, they take them to their graves. If they are in Egypt, they take them to the graves of Jews and Christians, or of these ʿUbaydīs, who sometimes claim descent from the Prophet, although they are not his descendants. They do not take horses to the graves of the prophets and the righteous, nor to the graves of ordinary Muslims. This is known from experience among soldiers and those among them who are knowledgeable.

The reason given for this is that the unbelievers are punished in their graves, and animals hear their cries, as the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, informed us. In the two Sahīh collections, it is related concerning the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace:

“He was riding his mule and passed some graves. It shied, nearly throwing him off, and he said, ‘These are the cries of Jews being punished in their graves.’”

When animals hear that dreadful sound, it produces enough heat within them to dispel the colic. The ignorant used to think that horses were walked near these people’s graves because of their religious devotion and merit. But then it became clear to them that horses were walked near the graves of Jews, Christians, Nusayrīs, and the like, rather than those of the prophets and the righteous. Scholars also explained that, in Egypt, Syria, and elsewhere, horses were not walked near the grave of anyone known for piety; rather, they were walked only near the graves of the wicked and the unbelievers. Thus, what had been unclear became evident.

Anyone familiar with the events of Islamic history and what took place between Islam’s supporters and its enemies among the unbelievers and hypocrites knows that these transgressors’ hostility towards the Islam with which Allah sent His Messenger was greater than that of the Tatars. He also knows that the esoteric knowledge they claimed was, in reality, the repudiation of the message with which Allah sent Muhammad—indeed, the repudiation of all the messengers—and that they do not acknowledge

---

## Page 16584

what the Messenger brought from Allah—neither his reports nor his commands—and that they are firmly intent on undermining his call, corrupting his religion, and killing his close followers and the followers of his family; and that their hostility towards Islam—indeed, towards all religions—is greater than that of the Jews and Christians.

For the Jews and Christians acknowledge the fundamental teachings brought by the messengers, such as affirming the Creator, the messengers, the revealed laws, and the Last Day. They nevertheless reject some of the scriptures and messengers, as Allah, glorified be He, says:

“Indeed, those who disbelieve in Allah and His messengers and wish to make a distinction between Allah and His messengers, saying, ‘We believe in some and disbelieve in others,’ and wish to adopt a way in between—”

“Those are truly the disbelievers, and We have prepared for the disbelievers a humiliating punishment.”

As for these Qarmatians, inwardly they disbelieve in all the scriptures and messengers. They hide this and conceal it from everyone except those they trust. They do not proclaim it as the People of the Book proclaim their religion, because, were they to disclose it, the vast majority of people on earth, Muslims and others alike, would recoil from them. They distinguish their own doctrine from that of the general public. Indeed, even those Rāfidīs who are not covert unbelievers distinguish their doctrine from that of the general public and advocate concealing their beliefs and practising dissimulation. A person among the Rāfidīs whose lineage is genuine may not be inwardly Muslim, nor be a covert unbeliever, but may instead be an ignorant innovator.

If these people, despite their genuine lineage and their Islam, conceal the innovations and desires they follow, while the majority of people oppose them, then what of the esoteric Qarmatians, whom the adherents of all religions—Muslims, Jews, and Christians—declare to be unbelievers?

---

## Page 16585

The only ones close to them are the “Peripatetic philosophers, the followers of Aristotle,” for there is considerable affinity between them and the Qarmatians. Thus, leading Qarmatians are found to be inwardly adherents of philosophy, such as Sinān, who was in Syria, and al-Tūsī, who was their minister at Alamūt, then became an astrologer to these people and the king of the unbelievers, and wrote a commentary on Ibn Sīnā’s Pointers. He was the one who advised the king of the unbelievers to kill the caliph. Among the unbelieving Turks, he became the head of those whom they call the “Dāsmīdiyya.”

These men and others like them know that the Qarmatians’ outward displays of religion, miraculous gifts, and the like are false. Yet a man may adhere to philosophy and join them because they agree with his outward acknowledgment of the messengers and revealed laws, while interpreting these in ways that are self-evidently contrary to what the messengers brought.

For these adherents of philosophy interpret what the messengers taught concerning faith in Allah and the Last Day through negation and denial, in keeping with their own doctrine. As for the revealed laws governing conduct, they do not reject them as the Qarmatians do. Rather, they consider them binding on the common people, while considering some of them binding on the elite—or not considering them binding.

They say that, in what they reported and commanded, the messengers did not convey the realities of things, but instead brought something conducive to the welfare of the common people, even if it was in fact false. Thus, every proponent of falsehood has chosen to resort to fraudulent tricks with the aim of benefiting the common people, as did Ibn Tūmart, known as al-Mahdī, whose doctrine concerning the divine attributes was that of the philosophers

---

## Page 16586

because he was broadly like them. Yet he was not a hypocrite who rejected the messengers and set aside the revealed laws, nor did he posit an inner meaning for the practical religious law that contradicted its outward meaning. Rather, he held some Jahmī views that accorded with those of the philosophers, and some views of the Khawārij, who advocate recourse to the sword and declare people unbelievers on account of sin.

These Qarmatians are, inwardly and in reality, greater unbelievers than the Jews and Christians. Outwardly, however, they profess Islam; indeed, they even claim descent from the Prophet’s family, esoteric knowledge unavailable to the prophets and saints, and that their imam is infallible.

Thus, outwardly they make some of the greatest claims to possessing the realities of faith, while inwardly they are among those who disbelieve most deeply in the Most Merciful. They are like the liars who claimed prophethood. Allah Most High says:

“Who does greater wrong than someone who fabricates a lie against Allah, or says, ‘Revelation has come to me,’ when nothing has been revealed to him, or someone who says, ‘I will send down the like of what Allah has sent down’?”

These people may make both kinds of claim.

Anyone who seeks to rival the truthful Messenger must fall into one of the following categories:

Either he makes a claim like the Messenger’s, saying, “Allah has sent me and sent revelation down to me,” thereby lying about Allah;

or he claims to receive revelation without naming its source, saying, for example, “I was told,” “I was called,” “I was addressed,” and the like, while lying. In this case, he has omitted the agent;

or he makes neither claim, but claims that he can produce the like of what the Messenger brought.

The basis of this classification is that, in claiming to rival the Messenger, he attributes what he claims either to Allah, to himself, or to no one.

---

## Page 16587

These people, who make claims like the Messenger’s, are greater unbelievers than the Jews and Christians. What, then, of the Qarmatians, who fabricate lies against Allah more grievously than Musaylima did, deviate concerning Allah’s names and signs more grievously than Musaylima did, and wage war against Allah and His Messenger more fiercely than Musaylima did? A detailed account of their condition would be lengthy, but these pages cannot accommodate more than this.

What I have described is the condition of their imams and leaders who understand the true nature of their doctrine. Undoubtedly, some of the Shia and Rāfida who have joined them neither know the reality of their concealed beliefs nor inwardly agree with them. Such a person is a follower of apostate heretics, offering them allegiance and support. He is like the followers of those who teach union between Creator and creation: they offer them allegiance, revere them, and support them without understanding the true nature of their doctrine of the unity of existence—that the Creator is the creation.

Thus, whoever is inwardly a Muslim but, out of ignorance, reveres the teachings of Ibn ʿArabī, Ibn Sabʿīn, Ibn al-Fārid, and similar advocates of union is one of them. The same applies to anyone who reveres those who profess indwelling and union. These people’s relationship to the Jahmiyya is like those others’ relationship to the Rāfida and the Jahmiyya. Yet the Qarmatians are far greater unbelievers than the advocates of union. Thus, at best, their ordinary followers are Rāfidī Jahmīs.

Among the ordinary followers of the advocates of union, however, are people who are neither Rāfidīs nor outright Jahmīs. Rather, they do not understand their words and believe them to be the words of saints who have attained true spiritual insight. A fuller exposition of this answer belongs elsewhere. And Allah knows best.

---

## Page 16588

He was also asked—may Allah Most High have mercy on him:

What do the esteemed scholars, the imams of the religion—may Allah be pleased with them all and help them manifest the clear truth and suppress the factions of those who promote falsehood—say concerning the Nusayriyya, who maintain that wine is lawful, that souls transmigrate, and that the world has existed eternally; who deny resurrection, the raising of the dead, Paradise, and Hell beyond this worldly life; and who hold that “the five prayers” refer to five names: ʿAlī, Hasan, Husayn, Muhsin, and Fātima? In their view, uttering these five names suffices in place of the ritual bath required by sexual impurity, ablution, and the remaining conditions and obligations of the five prayers.

They also hold that “fasting” refers to the names of thirty men and thirty women whom they enumerate in their books, but there is insufficient space here to list them. They maintain that their god, who created the heavens and the earth, is ʿAlī ibn Abī Tālib—may Allah be pleased with him. According to them, he is God in heaven and the imam on earth. In their view, the wisdom behind the manifestation of the divine nature in this human nature was to make himself familiar to his creatures and servants, teaching them how to know and worship him.

They further maintain that a Nusayrī does not become a believing Nusayrī with whom they will sit and drink wine, to whom they will reveal their secrets, and to whom they will marry their women, until his instructor addresses him. What they mean by this address is that they make him swear an oath to conceal his religion and acknowledge his shaykhs

---

## Page 16589

and the leading figures of his school; to give sincere counsel to no Muslim or anyone else except members of his own religion; and to recognize his lord and imam through his manifestations in his lights and cycles, thereby recognizing the transitions of the Name and the Meaning in every age and era.

According to them, at the beginning of humanity, Adam was the Name and Seth was the Meaning; Jacob was the Name and Joseph was the Meaning. They claim to find support for this conception in what the glorious Quran relates about Jacob and Joseph—peace and blessings be upon them both. They say:

“As for Jacob, he was the Name and could not go beyond his station, so he said:

‘I will ask my Lord to forgive you.’

“Joseph, however, was the sought-after Meaning, so he said:

‘No blame will there be upon you today.’

“He did not make the matter dependent on anyone else, because he knew that he was the deity who directs all affairs.”

They identify Moses as the Name and Joshua as the Meaning, saying: “The sun was made to return for Joshua when he commanded it, and it obeyed his command. Can the sun be made to return for anyone but its lord?”

They identify Solomon as the Name and Āsif as the Meaning, the Powerful, the All-Powerful. They say: “Solomon was unable to bring the throne of Bilqīs, whereas Āsif was able to do so, because Solomon was the outward form, while Āsif was the Meaning, the Powerful, the All-Powerful.” One of them composed the following:

“Abel, Seth, Joseph, Joshua, Āsif,
Simon al-Safā, Haydar.”

They enumerate the prophets and messengers one by one in this fashion until the time of the Messenger of Allah—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—saying: “Muhammad is the Name and ʿAlī is the Meaning.” They continue this enumeration in the same order through every age down to our own time. Thus, part of the substance of their religious initiation is that ʿAlī is the Lord and that Muhammad is the Veil

---

## Page 16590

and that Salmān is the Gate. One of their foremost leaders and learned men recited verses of his own composition during the year 700 AH, saying:

“I bear witness that there is no deity but Haydara, the balding, large-bellied one;
No Veil conceals him but Muhammad, the truthful, the trustworthy;
And no path leads to him but Salmān, the mighty, the steadfast.”

They say that this arrangement has always existed and will always continue. The same applies to the Five Orphans and the Twelve Chiefs, whose names are well known among them and recorded in their vile books. They maintain that these figures continually appear alongside the Lord, the Veil, and the Gate in every cycle and era, forever and ever, without interruption or end.

They say that the devil of devils is ʿUmar ibn al-Khattāb—may Allah be pleased with him—and that next to him in the hierarchy of devilry is Abū Bakr—may Allah be pleased with him—followed by ʿUthmān—may Allah be pleased with them all, honour them, and exalt their ranks far above the assertions of heretics and the pretensions of the various misguided and corrupt groups. They maintain that these figures likewise remain present at all times, in the order already described. Their corrupt doctrines have branches and details that all derive from these fundamental principles.

This accursed sect gained control of a large part of Syria. (They are well known and openly profess this creed. Their condition has been verified by everyone who has associated with them and come to know them, including discerning Muslims, their scholars, and ordinary people as well, at

---

## Page 16591

the present time; for their affairs were concealed from most people when the forsaken Franks controlled the coastal regions. When Islamic rule returned, their true condition was uncovered and their misguidance became apparent. The affliction they cause is very great indeed.

May a Muslim give someone in marriage to them or marry from among them? In these circumstances, is it lawful to eat the animals they slaughter, or not? What is the ruling on cheese made with rennet from animals they slaughter? What is the ruling on their utensils and clothing? May they be buried among Muslims, or not? May they be employed at the Muslims’ frontier strongholds and entrusted with them? Or must the ruler dismiss them and employ other competent Muslim men? Does he sin if he delays their expulsion, or may he proceed gradually while intending to do so?

If he employs them, whether or not he makes allotments to them, may he spend funds from the public treasury on them? If he does so, and part of someone’s stipulated pay remains unpaid, may the ruler withhold it from him and spend it on other Muslims or eligible recipients, or earmark it for that purpose? Are these courses of action permissible for him, or obligatory? Is it lawful to shed the blood of the aforementioned Nusayrīs and take their wealth, or not?

If the ruler—may Allah Most High support him—wages jihad against them by suppressing their falsehood, removes them from the Muslims’ fortresses, warns the Muslims against intermarrying with them and eating the animals they slaughter, compels them to fast and pray, and prevents them from openly practising their false religion, they being the unbelievers nearest to him: is that better and more greatly rewarded than preparing and lying in wait to fight the Tatars in their own lands and destroying the towns of

---

## Page 16592

Sīs and the lands of the Franks with their inhabitants still in them? Or is that better than his waging jihad against the aforementioned Nusayrīs while keeping guard? Is the reward of those who guard the coastal frontier posts against a feared Frankish attack greater, or does this carry a greater reward?

Must those who know these people and their doctrines expose their true condition and help to put an end to their falsehood and establish Islam among them—so that perhaps Allah Most High may guide some of them to Islam and make Muslims of their descendants and children after they leave that grievous unbelief—or is it permissible to disregard and neglect them? What is the merit of someone who strives to accomplish this, wages jihad for it, keeps guard for it and perseveres in it? Please explain this in detail; may you be rewarded and recompensed, if Allah Most High wills. He has power over all things. Allah is sufficient for us, and He is the best Disposer of affairs.

Shaykh al-Islām Taqī al-Dīn Abū al-‘Abbās Ahmad ibn Taymiyyah answered:

Praise be to Allah, Lord of all worlds. These people known as the Nusayrīs, together with all the other groups of esoteric Qarmatians, are greater disbelievers than the Jews and Christians—indeed, than many polytheists. The harm they cause to the community of Muhammad, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, is greater than that caused by warring unbelievers, such as the unbelievers among the Tatars and Franks and others. Before ignorant Muslims, they profess Shiism and allegiance to the Prophet’s household, whereas in reality they believe neither in Allah, His Messenger or His Book, nor in any command or prohibition, reward or punishment, Paradise or Hell, nor in any messenger before Muhammad, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, nor in any previous religion. Rather, they take the words of Allah and His Messenger, as known to Muslim scholars, and interpret them as referring to matters

---

## Page 16593

they fabricate, claiming that these constitute esoteric knowledge, both of the kind mentioned by the questioner and of other kinds. There is no limit to their claims involving deviation concerning Allah Most High’s names and signs and the distortion of the words of Allah Most High and His Messenger from their proper meanings.

Their aim is to deny faith and the laws of Islam by every possible means, while pretending that these matters have underlying realities known to them. These include what the questioner mentioned, as well as their claims that “the five daily prayers” mean knowing their secrets, “the obligatory fast” means concealing their secrets, and “pilgrimage to the Ancient House” means visiting their shaykhs; that “the two hands of Abū Lahab” are Abū Bakr and ‘Umar; and that “the great news” and “the clear record” refer to ‘Alī ibn Abī Tālib.

Their hostility to Islam and its followers is marked by well-known incidents and written works. Whenever they have the power, they shed Muslim blood. On one occasion, they killed pilgrims and threw them into the well of Zamzam. They also once seized the Black Stone, which remained in their possession for some time. They have killed so many Muslim scholars and shaykhs that Allah Most High alone knows their number. They have written numerous books containing what the questioner mentioned and other things besides. Muslim scholars, in turn, have written books exposing their secrets and tearing away their veils, explaining the unbelief, heresy and deviation they embrace, which make them greater disbelievers than the Jews, the Christians and the idol-worshipping Brahmins of India.

What the questioner has mentioned in describing them is only a small part of what scholars know about them. It is known to us that the Christians gained control of the coastal regions of the Levant only through them. They invariably side with every enemy of the Muslims; thus, they side with the Christians against the Muslims. And among

---

## Page 16594

the greatest calamities in their eyes are the Muslims’ conquest of the coastal regions and the defeat of the Christians. Indeed, the Muslims’ victory over the Tatars is also among their greatest calamities. Among their greatest occasions for celebration is when the Christians seize Muslim frontier strongholds—Allah Most High’s protection is sought against this.

The Muslim frontier strongholds had remained in Muslim hands until partway through the fourth century AH, including even the island of Cyprus—may Allah facilitate its conquest soon—which the Muslims conquered during the caliphate of the Commander of the Faithful, “ ‘Uthmān ibn ‘Affān,” may Allah be pleased with him. Its conqueror was “Mu‘āwiya ibn Abī Sufyān.” At that time, these people who oppose Allah and His Messenger grew numerous in the coastal regions and elsewhere, and the Christians seized the coast. Then, through them, the Christians seized noble Jerusalem and other places, for their conduct was among the principal causes of this.

Then Allah raised up Muslim rulers who fought in the cause of Allah Most High, “such as Nūr al-Dīn the Martyr and Saladin,” together with their followers. They wrested the coastal regions from the Christians and from those members of this group who were there, and they also conquered Egypt. This group had controlled Egypt for some two hundred years and had allied themselves with the Christians. The Muslims fought them until they conquered the lands, and from that time onward the call to Islam spread throughout Egypt and the Levant.

Furthermore, the Tatars entered Muslim lands and killed the caliph of Baghdad and other Muslim rulers only with this group’s assistance and support. For Hūlāgū’s astrologer, who had been their vizier, was “al-Nasīr

---

## Page 16595

al-Tūsī,” who had served as their vizier at Alamūt and was the one who ordered that the caliph be killed and these people be placed in power. They have “designations” known among Muslims: sometimes they are called “heretics,” sometimes “Qarmatians,” sometimes “Bātiniyya (esotericists),” sometimes “Ismā‘īliyya,” sometimes “Nusayriyya,” sometimes “Khurramiyya,” and sometimes “Muhammira.” Some of these names encompass them all, while others apply only to particular groups among them, just as Islam and faith encompass all Muslims, while some Muslims have a particular designation based on ancestry, doctrine, locality, or something else.

A full explanation of their aims would be lengthy. As the scholars have said of them: “Outwardly, their doctrine is that of the Rāfidīs; inwardly, it is pure unbelief.” The truth about them is that they believe in none of the prophets and messengers: neither Noah, Abraham, Moses, Jesus, nor Muhammad—may Allah’s blessings and peace be upon them all—and in none of Allah’s revealed Books: neither the Torah, the Gospel, nor the Quran. Nor do they acknowledge that the world has a Creator who created it, that He has prescribed a religion to be followed, or that He has an abode other than this world in which He recompenses people for their deeds.

---

## Page 16596

At times they base their teachings on the doctrines of the natural philosophers or the metaphysicians; at other times, on the teachings of the Magians who worship light. To this they add Rāfidī doctrine.

They seek support for this in prophetic teachings, either through fabricated statements they transmit—as when they attribute to the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, the saying:

“The first thing Allah created was the intellect.”

The hadith is fabricated by the unanimous agreement of hadith scholars. Its actual wording is:

“When Allah created the intellect, He said to it, ‘Come forward,’ and it came forward. Then He said to it, ‘Go back,’ and it went back.”

They alter its wording to say, “The first thing Allah created was the intellect,” to bring it into agreement with the assertion of Aristotle’s followers among the philosophers that the first emanation from the Necessary Existent is the intellect.

Or they take words authentically transmitted from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and distort their meaning, as do the authors of the Epistles of the Brethren of Purity and others like them, who are among their leading authorities. Much of their falsehood has infiltrated the thinking of many Muslims and gained acceptance among them, even finding its way into the books of groups associated with learning and religion, although they do not share the fundamental unbelief of these people.

These people disclose their accursed mission, which they call “the Guiding Mission,” in several stages. They call the final stage “the Supreme Disclosure and the Supreme Law.” The substance of the Supreme Disclosure is denial of the Creator, exalted is He, and mockery of Him and of those who acknowledge Him—to the point that one of them may write Allah’s name on the sole of his foot. It also includes denial of His revealed laws, His religion, and what the prophets brought, along with the claim that the prophets were people like themselves seeking power: some pursued it skillfully, while others went wrong in

---

## Page 16597

pursuing it and ended up being killed. They place Muhammad and Moses in the first category, and the Messiah in the second.

It contains mockery of prayer, zakat, fasting and pilgrimage, and declares marriage to women within the prohibited degrees of kinship and all other indecencies lawful, in ways too lengthy to describe. They have signals and forms of address by which they recognize one another. When they are in Muslim lands where believers are numerous, they may escape the notice of those unfamiliar with them. But where they themselves are numerous, even ordinary people recognize them, let alone those with specialized knowledge.

Muslim scholars have unanimously agreed that intermarriage with these people is impermissible: a man may neither give a woman under his guardianship in marriage to one of them nor marry a woman from among them. Nor is it permissible to eat animals they slaughter.

As for “cheese made with their rennet,” scholars hold two well-known views, as they do regarding rennet from carrion generally, or from animals slaughtered by Magians or by the Franks, who are said not to slaughter animals in the prescribed manner.

The position of Abū Hanīfa, and of Ahmad according to one of the two reports from him, is that this cheese is lawful. According to this view, rennet from carrion is ritually pure, because the rennet does not die when the animal dies, and contact with the impure stomach containing it does not render it impure.

The position of Mālik, al-Shāfi‘ī and Ahmad, according to the other report from him, is that this cheese is ritually impure, because they regard the rennet as impure: in their view, both the milk and the rennet of an animal that has died without valid slaughter are impure. An animal slaughtered by someone whose slaughtered animals may not be eaten is treated as carrion.

The proponents of each view cite reports they transmit from the Companions. Those holding the first view report that the Companions ate cheese made by the Magians. The proponents of the second view

---

## Page 16598

report that they ate what they believed to be cheese made by Christians. This is a matter open to juristic interpretation; someone who relies on scholarly authority may follow a scholar who gives a fatwa according to either view.

As for “their vessels and clothing,” these are treated like the vessels and clothing of the Magians, according to the established positions of the leading jurists. The correct view is that their vessels should not be used until they have been washed. Since the animals they slaughter are carrion, the vessels they use inevitably come into contact with the meat they cook, rendering them ritually impure. However, vessels that are not thought likely to have come into contact with impurity may be used without washing, such as milk vessels in which they do not put their cooked food, or which they wash before putting milk into them. ‘Umar ibn al-Khattāb, may Allah be pleased with him, performed ablution using water from a Christian woman’s jar. Thus, something whose impurity is doubtful is not deemed impure on the basis of doubt alone.

They may not be buried in Muslim cemeteries, nor may funeral prayers be offered for those of them who die. Allah, Glorified and Exalted, forbade His Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, to offer funeral prayers for the hypocrites, such as ‘Abd Allāh ibn Ubayy and others like him. They outwardly observed prayer, zakat, fasting and jihad alongside the Muslims, and did not openly profess any doctrine contrary to Islam, but concealed such beliefs. Allah said:

“Never offer a funeral prayer for any of them who dies, nor stand at his grave. They disbelieved in Allah and His Messenger and died defiantly disobedient.”

What, then, of these people who, in addition to their heresy and hypocrisy, openly display unbelief and impiety?

As for employing such people at the Muslims’ frontier posts, in their fortresses or in their army, this is a major sin. It is like employing wolves to tend sheep, for they are among the most treacherous of people

---

## Page 16599

toward the Muslims and their rulers. They are the most eager of all people to bring ruin upon the realm and the state, and they are worse than a traitor within the army. A traitor may have some particular aim involving either the army commander or the enemy, whereas these people direct their designs against the religious community, its Prophet, its religion, its kings, its scholars, its common people and its elite. They are the most eager of all people to surrender fortresses to the Muslims’ enemy, to turn the troops against the ruler and to lead them out of obedience to him.

The authorities must remove them from the military registers; they must not be retained at frontier posts or elsewhere, for the harm they cause at the frontier is greater still. They must be replaced, as needed, with men who can be trusted to uphold the religion of Islam and to remain sincere toward Allah, His Messenger, the Muslim leaders and the Muslims at large. Indeed, if a ruler will not employ someone who betrays him, even if that person is a Muslim, how could he employ someone who betrays all Muslims? He may not delay this duty when he is able to carry it out. Whenever he can replace them, he must do so.

If, however, they have been employed and have performed the work stipulated for them, they are entitled either to the agreed payment or to the customary remuneration for equivalent work, since they were engaged under a contract to perform it. If the contract was valid, the agreed payment is due; if it was invalid, the customary remuneration for equivalent work is due. If their employment does not fall within the category of

---

## Page 16600

a binding contract of hire, it falls instead within the category of a revocable agreement promising payment for a task. However, employing these people is impermissible, so the contract is invalid, and they are entitled only to the value of their work. If they have performed no work of value, they are entitled to nothing. Their blood, however, may lawfully be shed and their property taken.

If they profess repentance, the scholars disagree over whether it should be accepted. Those who accept their repentance, provided they commit themselves to the law of Islam, allow them to retain their property. According to those who do not accept it, their property is not transferred to heirs belonging to their own sect; rather, it becomes revenue for the public treasury. Yet when these people are apprehended, they profess repentance, for their doctrine rests upon dissimulation and concealing their true beliefs. Some of them are known, while others may not be.

The course to follow is therefore to take precautions concerning them. They must not be allowed to remain together, bear arms or serve as combatants. They must be required to observe the prescribed practices of Islam, including the five daily prayers and recitation of the Quran. Someone should remain among them to teach them the religion of Islam, and they must be kept apart from their own teacher.

When Abū Bakr al-Siddīq—may Allah be pleased with him—and the other Companions overcame the apostates, and the latter came to him, al-Siddīq said to them:

“Choose either a war that drives you out or a humiliating peace.”

They said: “O successor to the Messenger of Allah, we know what a war that drives us out means, but what is a humiliating peace?”

He said: “You shall pay blood money for our slain, but we shall pay none for yours. You shall testify that our slain are in Paradise and yours are in the Fire. We shall divide what we have seized of your property, and you shall return what you have seized of ours. Your armour and weapons shall be taken from you, and you shall be forbidden to ride

---

## Page 16601

horses, and you shall be left to tend camels until Allah makes a course of action clear to the successor of His Messenger and the believers following your apostasy.”

The Companions agreed with him on this, except for requiring blood money for the Muslims who had been killed. For ʿUmar ibn al-Khattāb—may Allah be pleased with him—said to him: “These men were killed in Allah’s cause, and their reward rests with Allah.” That is, they were martyrs, so no blood money was due for them. They therefore agreed upon ʿUmar’s position on this matter.

What the Companions agreed upon is the position of the leading scholars, while the matter over which they differed is likewise disputed among the scholars. The majority hold that no blood money is due for anyone killed by apostates fighting as an organized force, in accordance with the Companions’ eventual agreement. This is the position of Abū Hanīfa and of Ahmad according to one of the two reports from him. The position of al-Shāfiʿī and of Ahmad according to the other report is the initial view.

The measures the Companions applied to those apostates after their return to Islam should likewise be applied to anyone who professes Islam but remains manifestly suspect. Such a person is to be prevented from serving as a horseman, bearing weapons or wearing the armour worn by combatants. Nor is anyone who is a Jew or a Christian to be retained in the army. They must be required to observe the prescribed practices of Islam until the good or evil they do becomes apparent.

Any of their leaders in misguidance who professes repentance is to be removed from their midst and sent to Muslim lands where they have no prominence. Then either Allah Most High will guide him, or he will die in his hypocrisy without harming the Muslims.

There is no doubt that jihad against these people and enforcing the prescribed punishments upon them are among the greatest acts of obedience and the most important obligations. This is superior to jihad against those polytheists and People of the Book who do not fight the Muslims, for jihad against these people is akin to jihad against the apostates, and al-Siddīq

---

## Page 16602

and the rest of the Companions began by fighting the apostates before fighting the disbelievers among the People of the Book. Fighting these people safeguards the Muslim lands already conquered and brings within Islam those who wish to leave it. Fighting those polytheists and People of the Book who have not fought us, by contrast, further extends the ascendancy of the religion. Preserving one’s capital takes precedence over making a profit.

Moreover, the harm these people cause the Muslims is greater than that caused by the others. Indeed, their harm is of the same kind as that caused by the polytheists and People of the Book who fight the Muslims. The harm they inflict upon many people’s religion is more severe than that caused by those warring polytheists and People of the Book.

Every Muslim must fulfil his duty in this matter to the extent of his ability. No one may conceal what he knows about them; rather, he must disclose it and make it known so that the Muslims may understand their true condition. No one may help them remain in military or other service. No one may remain silent instead of taking the action against them that Allah and His Messenger have commanded. Nor may anyone forbid carrying out what Allah and His Messenger have commanded. This is among the greatest forms of enjoining right, forbidding wrong, and jihad in the cause of Allah Most High. Allah Most High said to His Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace:

“O Prophet, strive against the disbelievers and the hypocrites, and be firm with them.”

These people fall into one of those two categories: disbelievers or hypocrites. Whoever helps restrain their evil and guide them as far as possible will receive a reward and recompense known only to Allah Most High. For the primary objective is to guide them, as Allah says:

---

## Page 16603

Most High:

“You are the best community ever raised for mankind.”

Abū Hurayrah said: “You are the best people for mankind: you bring them in fetters and chains until you bring them into Islam.”

The purpose of jihad, enjoining right, and forbidding wrong is to guide people, as far as possible, to what benefits them in this life and the Hereafter. Whoever Allah guides finds happiness in this world and the Hereafter; whoever remains unguided, Allah restrains his harm from reaching others.

It is well known that jihad, enjoining right, and forbidding wrong are the best of deeds. As the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Islam is the foundation of the matter, prayer is its pillar, and jihad in the cause of Allah Most High is its pinnacle.”

An authentic hadith reports that he, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“In Paradise there are a hundred degrees which Allah, Mighty and Majestic, has prepared for those who strive in His cause. The distance between one degree and the next is like the distance between heaven and earth.”

He, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, also said:

“Guarding the frontier for a day and a night in the cause of Allah is better than fasting for a month and spending its nights in prayer.”

Whoever dies while guarding the frontier dies as one striving in Allah’s cause. The reward for his deeds continues to accrue to him, his provision from Paradise continues to reach him, and he is kept safe from trial.

Jihad is better than Hajj and ‘umrah, as Allah Most High says:

“Do you regard providing water for pilgrims and maintaining the Sacred Mosque as equal to the deeds of those who believe in Allah and the Last Day and strive in Allah’s cause? They are not equal in Allah’s sight. Allah does not guide the wrongdoing people.”

“Those who believe, emigrate, and strive in Allah’s cause with their wealth and their lives are greater in rank with Allah. It is they who are successful.”

“Their Lord gives them glad tidings of mercy from Him, His pleasure, and Gardens in which they will enjoy lasting bliss.”

“They will remain there forever. Indeed, with Allah is an immense reward.”

All praise belongs to Allah, Lord of all worlds. May His blessings and peace be upon the best of His creation, our master Muhammad, and upon all his family and Companions.

---

## Page 16604

He—may Allah Most High have mercy on him—was asked about the “Druze” and the “Nusayrīs”: What is the ruling concerning them?

He replied:

These “Druze” and “Nusayrīs” are disbelievers by the consensus of the Muslims. It is unlawful to eat animals they slaughter or to marry their women. Indeed, they may not even be granted protected status upon payment of the jizyah tax, for they are apostates from Islam: they are neither Muslims, Jews, nor Christians. They do not acknowledge the obligation of the five daily prayers, fasting Ramadan, or Hajj, nor do they acknowledge the prohibition of what Allah and His Messenger have forbidden, such as carrion, wine, and other things. Even if they outwardly profess the two testimonies of faith while holding these beliefs, they are disbelievers by the consensus of the Muslims.

As for the “Nusayrīs,” they are the followers of Abū Shuʿayb Muhammad ibn Nusayr. He was one of the extremists who say that ʿAlī is a god. They recite:

“I bear witness that there is no god but … Haydarah, with receding hair and ample belly;
No veil before him but … Muhammad, the truthful and trustworthy;
No path to him but … Salmān, mighty and steadfast.”

As for the “Druze,” they are the followers of Hashtakīn al-Durzī, who was one of al-Hākim’s retainers. Al-Hākim sent him to the people of Wādī Taym Allāh ibn Thaʿlabah, and he called them to believe in al-Hākim’s divinity, and they call him

---

## Page 16605

“the Creator, the All-Knowing,” and swear by him. They belong to the Ismāʿīlīs, who hold that Muhammad ibn Ismāʿīl abrogated the religious law of Muhammad ibn ʿAbd Allāh. Their disbelief is greater than that of the extremists. They maintain that the world has existed from eternity, deny the resurrection, and reject Islam’s obligations and prohibitions. They belong to the esotericist Qarmatians, whose disbelief is greater than that of the Jews, Christians, and Arab polytheists. Their ultimate aim is to be “philosophers” following the doctrine of Aristotle and others like him, or “Magians.” Their doctrine combines the views of the philosophers and the Magians, while they hypocritically profess adherence to Shiism. Allah knows best.

Shaykh al-Islām—may Allah have mercy on him—also said, in refutation of brief statements from groups of the “Druze”:

The disbelief of these people is a matter on which Muslims do not differ. Indeed, whoever doubts their disbelief is himself a disbeliever like them. They have neither the status of the People of the Book nor that of the polytheists; rather, they are misguided disbelievers. It is therefore unlawful to eat their food; their women are taken captive and their property seized. They are heretics and apostates whose repentance is not accepted. Rather, they are to be killed wherever they are found and cursed as described. They may not be employed as guards, gatekeepers, or custodians.

Their scholars and pious men must be killed lest they lead others astray. It is forbidden to spend the night alongside them in their houses or travelling parties, to walk with them, or to accompany their funeral processions when their death is known. It is forbidden for those governing the Muslims to neglect Allah’s command to enforce the prescribed punishments upon them, in whatever manner the person enforcing them—not the person subjected to them—sees fit. Allah is the One whose help is sought, and upon Him we rely.

---

## Page 16606

He—may Allah Most High have mercy on him—was also asked:

“Who are these ‘Qalandariyya’ who shave their beards, and to which groups do they belong? What do you say about their belief that the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, fed their shaykh Qalandar grapes and spoke to him in a non-Arabic tongue?”

He replied:

These ‘Qalandariyya’ who shave their beards are people of misguidance and ignorance. Most of them disbelieve in Allah and His Messenger: they do not regard prayer and fasting as obligatory, do not consider unlawful what Allah and His Messenger have forbidden, and do not follow the religion of truth. Indeed, many of them are greater disbelievers than the Jews and Christians. They belong neither to the Muslim community nor to the protected non-Muslim communities. Some among them may be Muslims, but are misguided innovators or wicked sinners.

Whoever claims that ‘Qalandar’ existed in the time of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, has lied and fabricated a tale. Rather, it has been said that this group originated as Persian ascetics who, after fulfilling their religious obligations and avoiding forbidden acts, pursued whatever brought comfort to their hearts. This is how Shaykh Abū Hafs al-Suhrawardī described them in his ‘Awārif. Later, however, they abandoned their obligations and committed forbidden acts. They were like the

---

## Page 16607

“Malāmiyya,” who concealed their good deeds and outwardly adopted appearances not associated with righteousness, such as dressing like the wealthy and wearing a turban. This comes close to what is right, and a person who does so is rewarded for his intention. Then others emerged who engaged in practices deemed reprehensible by Islamic law. Matters then went further: some committed forbidden acts, including indecencies and other wrongdoing, and abandoned obligatory duties, claiming that they were thereby engaging in “blame-seeking practices.” They were indeed right insofar as they deserved blame, censure and punishment from Allah in this world and the Hereafter. All of them must be punished and prevented from maintaining this accursed outward practice, just as anyone who openly displays religious innovation or wickedness must be.

This is not confined to them. Rather, it applies to anyone among religious devotees, students of jurisprudence, worshippers, those who profess poverty, ascetics, speculative theologians and philosophers, as well as those who agree with them among rulers, the wealthy, scribes, accountants, physicians, government officials and ordinary people, who departs from the guidance and religion of truth with which Allah sent His Messenger: someone who does not affirm everything Allah has conveyed through His Messenger, does not regard as forbidden what Allah and His Messenger have forbidden, or follows a religion that conflicts, inwardly and outwardly, with the religion with which Allah sent His Messenger.

Examples include someone who believes that his shaykh provides for him, grants him victory, guides him, comes to his rescue or assists him; someone who worships his shaykh or supplicates to him and prostrates before him; someone who considers his shaykh superior to the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, either absolutely or in some particular virtue that brings one nearer to Allah Most High; or someone who believes that he or his shaykh has no need to follow the Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. All such people are disbelievers if they disclose these beliefs, and hypocrites if they conceal them.

---

## Page 16608

Although these groups have become numerous in our time, this is due to the scarcity of those who call people to knowledge and faith, and the waning influence of the prophetic message in most lands. Most of these people do not possess enough of the legacy of the prophetic message and the inheritance of prophethood to recognize right guidance, and many have never received it at all.

In times and places where prophetic guidance has faded, a person is rewarded for whatever little faith he possesses. In such circumstances, Allah forgives those against whom the proof has not been established for things He does not forgive those against whom it has been established. This is illustrated by the well-known hadith:

“A time will come upon people when they will know neither prayer nor fasting, neither Hajj nor ‘umrah, except for elderly men and women, who will say: ‘We remember our fathers saying: There is no god but Allah.’ Hudhayfah ibn al-Yamān was asked: ‘What benefit will “There is no god but Allah” be to them?’ He replied: ‘It will save them from the Fire.’”

The underlying principle is that a statement constituting disbelief according to the Quran, the Sunnah and scholarly consensus is declared to be disbelief in general terms, as indicated by the evidence of Islamic law. Judgments concerning “faith” are received from Allah and His Messenger; they are not matters for people to decide according to their conjectures and desires.

Yet not every individual who makes such a statement is to be judged a disbeliever: the conditions for declaring him a disbeliever must first be established in his case, and the impediments to doing so must be absent. An example is someone who says that wine or usury is lawful because he has only recently embraced Islam or grew up in a remote desert region. Another is someone who hears words and rejects them without believing them to be part of the Quran or the hadiths of the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. Some of the early generations likewise rejected certain things until it was established to their satisfaction that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, had said them. Similarly, the Companions had doubts concerning certain matters, such as seeing Allah and other matters

---

## Page 16609

until they asked the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, about them. Another example is the man who said:

“When I die, grind me to dust and scatter me in the sea; perhaps I may elude Allah,” and similar cases.

Such people are not to be declared disbelievers until the proof has been established against them through the prophetic message, as Allah Most High says:

“So that people would have no argument against Allah after the messengers.”

Allah has pardoned this community for mistakes and forgetfulness. We have discussed the principles underlying this answer at length in their respective places, and this fatwa does not allow for further elaboration. Allah knows best.

He—may Allah have mercy on him—was asked:

About someone who believes that the heavenly bodies influence existence, or says that he has a star in the sky and that he prospers when it is auspicious and suffers when it is otherwise. He cites as evidence the words of Allah Most High:

“And those who arrange affairs,”

and His words:

“So I swear by the positions of the stars.”

He says that this is the craft of Idrīs, peace be upon him, and claims that the star of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, was in Scorpio and Mars. Is this part of the religion of Islam or not? Even if it is not part of the religion, what is required of someone who says it? Are those who denounce such people counted among those who enjoin what is right and forbid what is wrong, or not?

He answered:

Praise be to Allah. The stars are among Allah’s signs: they point to Him, glorify Him, and prostrate themselves before Him, as He Most High says:

“Do you not see that to Allah prostrate all who are in the heavens and all who are on earth

---

## Page 16610

and the sun, the moon, the stars, the mountains, the trees, the animals, and many people.” Then He says:

“But many have deserved punishment.”

This distinction makes clear that He did not mean by prostration merely their pointing to His lordship, as some groups claim. All created things share in pointing to Him in this way: all people do so, yet He has distinguished between them. It follows that this prostration is a further expression of the kind peculiar to the believer, distinguishing him from the disbeliever who has deserved punishment.

Nevertheless, He, glorified be He, has placed benefits for His servants in these heavenly bodies and subjected them to their service, as He Most High says:

“He has subjected the sun and the moon to your service, both constant in their courses, and has subjected the night and the day to your service.”

He also says:

“And the sun, the moon, and the stars, subjected by His command.”

And He says:

“He has subjected to your service whatever is in the heavens and whatever is on earth—all from Him.”

Among their evident benefits are the heat and cold, night and day, ripening of fruits, and creation of animals, plants, and minerals that He, glorified be He, brings about through the sun. Likewise, through these heavenly bodies He brings about moistening and drying for people, along with other observable effects. Similarly, He has given fire the properties of illumination and burning, and water those of purification and providing drink. These and similar things are among His blessings that He mentions in His Book, as He Most High says:

“And We send down purifying water from the sky, so that through it We may bring life to a dead land and give drink to the many livestock and people We have created.”

Allah has stated in several places that He gives life to some of His creatures through others, as He Most High says:

“So that through it We may bring life to a dead land.”

And as He says:

“It is He who sends the winds as bearers of glad tidings ahead of His mercy, until, when they have borne heavy clouds, We drive them to a dead land, send down water there, and through it bring forth

---

## Page 16611

fruits of every kind.” And He says:

“And the water Allah sends down from the sky, by which He revives the earth after its death and disperses throughout it every kind of creature.”

Thus, any speculative theologian who says that Allah brings about these things when these means are present, but not through them, uses language contrary to Allah’s Book and observable realities. Likewise, anyone who claims that these means act independently is a polytheist who contradicts both reason and religion.

He, glorified be He, has mentioned some of the benefits of the stars in His Book: they provide guidance through the darkness of land and sea. He has also stated that they adorn the lowest heaven and that the devils are pelted with stars. The stars with which the devils are pelted, however, are of a different kind from the fixed stars in the sky by which people navigate. The latter do not leave their places, unlike the former. Their natures differ, even though the designation “stars” encompasses both, just as the terms “creature” and “living being” encompass angels, human beings, beasts, flies, and mosquitoes.

Authentic reports from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, whose authenticity the scholars agree upon, establish that he commanded prayer when the sun or moon is eclipsed. He also commanded supplication, seeking forgiveness, charity, and the freeing of slaves, and said:

“The sun and the moon are two of Allah’s signs. They are not eclipsed because of anyone’s death or life.”

Another narration states:

“Two of Allah’s signs, through which He inspires fear in His servants.”

He said this to refute the claim of some ignorant people that the sun had been eclipsed because of the death of Ibrāhīm, the son of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. The sun was eclipsed on the day of his death, and when it was eclipsed, some people supposed that its eclipse was due to his death and that his death was

---

## Page 16612

the cause of its eclipse, just as the death of certain great men brings calamities upon people. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, therefore explained that neither the sun nor the moon is eclipsed because of the death or life of anyone on earth.

He denied that death or life has any effect on eclipses of the sun and moon, and stated that they are among Allah’s signs, through which He inspires fear in His servants. He thus mentioned that one purpose behind this is to inspire fear in His servants, just as other signs do: strong winds, earthquakes, drought, persistent rains, and similar causes that may bring punishment. Allah punished nations with wind, the blast, and the flood. Allah Most High says:

“So We seized each for his sin. Among them were those upon whom We sent a storm of stones; among them were those seized by the blast; among them were those whom We caused the earth to swallow; and among them were those We drowned.”

He also says:

“And We gave Thamūd the she-camel as a clear sign, but they wronged her. We send signs only as a warning.”

The statement that He thereby inspires fear in His servants indicates that such a phenomenon may be a cause of punishment descending, as with fierce, stormy winds. This can only be so if Allah has made it a cause of what befalls the earth.

Thus, if someone says, “They have an effect,” meaning what is known about these phenomena through sense perception and other means, this is true. But Allah has commanded acts of worship that avert from us the harm sent through them, just as He commanded the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, at the time of an eclipse, to pray, give charity, supplicate, seek forgiveness, and free slaves. Likewise, when the wind blew, he, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, would move back and forth, and his demeanor would change. He instructed that, when it blew, one should say:

“O Allah, we ask You for the good of this wind and the good of what

---

## Page 16613

it was sent with, and we seek refuge in You from the evil of this wind and the evil of what it was sent with.” He also said:

“The wind is of Allah’s mercy. It brings mercy and brings punishment, so do not revile it. Rather, ask Allah for its good and seek refuge in Allah from its evil.”

He thus explained that it brings mercy and brings punishment, and instructed us to ask Allah for its good and seek refuge in Allah from its evil.

This, then, is the Sunnah concerning the causes of good and evil: the servant should respond to manifest causes of good and righteous deeds by doing that through which Allah brings good, and to manifest causes of evil by performing acts of worship through which Allah wards off evil from him. As for hidden causes, the servant is not commanded to go to great lengths to discover them. Rather, if he does what Allah has commanded and refrains from what He has forbidden, Allah will spare him the burden of evil and facilitate the means of good for him:

“And whoever fears Allah, He will make a way out for him and provide for him from where he does not expect. And whoever relies upon Allah, He is sufficient for him. Indeed, Allah will accomplish His purpose. Allah has appointed a measure for everything.”

Allah Most High has said of those who practice magic to obtain worldly benefits:

“They followed what the devils recited during the reign of Solomon. Solomon did not disbelieve”—through to His words—“Had they believed and feared Allah, a reward from Allah would have been better, if only they knew.”

He, glorified be He, thus stated that whoever acquires this in exchange knows that he will have no share in the Hereafter. He merely hopes for what he supposes to be its benefit in this world, just as those who practice magic connected with celestial bodies and other things hope thereby to gain such things as authority and wealth.

Then He said:

“Had they believed and feared Allah, a reward from Allah

---

## Page 16614

would have been better, if only they knew.” He thus explained that faith and fear of Allah are better for them in this world and the Hereafter. Allah Most High said:

“Indeed, the allies of Allah will have no fear, nor will they grieve—those who believed and were mindful of Him,”

and the passage continues. He also said in the account of Joseph:

“Thus We established Joseph in the land, free to settle wherever he wished. We bestow Our mercy upon whom We will, and We do not let the reward of those who do good go to waste. And the reward of the Hereafter is better for those who believe and are mindful of Allah.”

He thus declared that the reward of the Hereafter is better for believers who fear Allah than the dominion and wealth they are given in this world, such as Joseph was given. In numerous verses, He, glorified be He, has also declared the evil outcome, in this world and the Hereafter, of those who abandon faith and fear of Allah. Hence His statement:

“The magician will never succeed, wherever he goes.”

The successful person is one who attains what he seeks and escapes what he fears. The magician achieves neither. In the Sunan of Abū Dāwūd, the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, is reported to have said:

“Whoever acquires a branch of astrology has acquired a branch of magic.”

Magic is forbidden by the Quran, the Sunnah, and scholarly consensus. The astrology that constitutes magic is of two kinds. The first is theoretical: inferring events from the movements of the stars. This is akin to seeking decisions through divining arrows. The second is practical: what they describe in terms of celestial forces in conjunction with receptive earthly forces, as in talismans and the like. This is among the highest forms of magic. Everything Allah and His Messenger have forbidden causes more harm than benefit.

---

## Page 16615

As for the second, even if someone imagines that it provides advance knowledge of events and that this is beneficial, ignorance in this regard is less serious, and its harm outweighs its benefit.

Thus, specialists and ordinary people alike have learned through experience and widely transmitted reports that astrologers’ predictions prove false many times more often than they prove true. In this respect, they are a kind of soothsayer. It is authentically reported that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, was told:

“Some of us visit soothsayers.”

He replied, “They are of no account.”

They said, “O Messenger of Allah, sometimes they tell us something that turns out to be true.”

The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, replied, “That word of truth is heard by a jinn, who conveys it into the ear of his ally.”

He also related:

“When Allah decrees a matter, the angels beat their wings in submission to His words, which sound like a chain upon smooth stone. Then, when the fear has departed from their hearts, they ask, ‘What did your Lord say?’ They reply, ‘The truth.’ The inhabitants of each heaven inform those of the adjoining heaven, until the news reaches the lowest heaven. There, the eavesdroppers are stationed one above another. Sometimes one hears the word before a shooting star overtakes him; sometimes the shooting star overtakes him after he has passed it on.”

He, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said, “If only they conveyed the matter as it actually was! But they add a hundred lies to that word.”

The same is true of astrologers. Indeed, I spoke with them in Damascus, and their leading figures came to me. I demonstrated the falsity of their craft through rational arguments whose validity they acknowledged. One of their leaders said:

“By Allah, we tell a hundred lies before we speak one word of truth.”

---

## Page 16616

This is because their discipline rests on the premise that celestial motions cause events, and that knowledge of the cause entails knowledge of the effect. Yet this holds only when one knows the complete cause whose effect invariably follows. At most—if they know anything at all—these people know only a very small part of the many causes involved. They know neither the remaining causes nor the necessary conditions nor the impediments.

They are like someone who knows that the sun rises overhead in summer, producing intense heat, and seeks to infer from this, for example, that the grapes on a particular piece of land will then become raisins. This assumes that there are grapes there, that they ripen, and that their owner spreads them out in the sun during the heat so that they become raisins. Although this often happens, inferring it from the sun’s heat alone betrays profound ignorance. There may or may not be grapes there; those vines may bear fruit if tended, or they may not. The grapes may be eaten fresh, pressed, stolen, or made into raisins, and so forth.

There is abundant evidence demonstrating the falsity of this craft and its prohibition, but this is not the place to discuss it. It is authentically reported in Sahīh Muslim that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Whoever visits a diviner and asks him about something will have no prayer accepted for forty days.”

The term “diviner” has been said to be a general designation encompassing soothsayers, astrologers, geomancers, and others who claim advance knowledge through such methods. Even if it were said that, linguistically, the term designates only some of these types, the others would still fall within its scope by virtue of the generality of its underlying meaning, as has been said concerning the terms “wine,” “gambling,” and the like.

---

## Page 16617

As for those who deny that any movements of celestial bodies or other phenomena can act as causes, this too is a claim made without knowledge. They have no evidence for it, whether from revelation or otherwise. Indeed, the revealed texts indicate the contrary, as in the hadith reported in the Sunan from ‘Ā’ishah, may Allah be pleased with her:

“The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, looked at the moon and said: ‘O ‘Ā’ishah, seek refuge in Allah from the evil of this, for this is the darkness when it settles.’”

Likewise, as mentioned earlier, in the hadith concerning eclipses he stated that “Allah uses them both to frighten His servants.” It has already become clear that the Prophet’s statement, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, “They are not eclipsed because of anyone’s death or life,” means that death does not cause an eclipse. It thus denies an efficient cause. This is like the other hadith in Sahīh Muslim, reported by Ibn ‘Abbās from some men of the Ansār, who were with the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, when a shooting star was hurled forth and shone brightly. He said:

“What did you use to say about this in the days of ignorance?”

They replied: “We used to say, ‘A great person has been born tonight, or a great person has died.’”

He said: “They are not hurled because of anyone’s death or life. Rather, when Allah decrees a matter, the bearers of the Throne glorify Him.”

He then related the rest of the hadith concerning those who eavesdrop. Thus, the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, denied that these stars are hurled because a great person has been born or has died; rather, they are hurled because of the devils who eavesdrop.

Both hadiths therefore indicate that people’s deaths and lives do not cause eclipses of the sun or moon, or the hurling of shooting stars. Nevertheless, the death of certain people may occasion an event in the heavens, as is established in the authentic hadith collections: “The Throne—the Throne of the Most Merciful—shook at the death of Sa‘d

---

## Page 16618

ibn Mu‘ādh.” As for an eclipse or another such phenomenon possibly causing an event on earth, such as a punishment that brings death or something else, the hadith itself affirms this.

What the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, stated does not conflict with eclipses occurring at fixed times: a solar eclipse occurs only at the end of the month, on the night when the moon is hidden, while a lunar eclipse occurs only in the middle of the month, during the nights of the full moon.

Anyone among those studying jurisprudence or among ordinary people who claims otherwise does so because he lacks knowledge of astronomical calculation. Hence, past and future eclipses can be determined, just as past and future new moons can be determined, since all of this follows a precise reckoning. As Allah Most High says:

“He made the night for rest, and the sun and the moon for reckoning.”

And He says:

“The sun and the moon move by precise calculation.”

And He says:

“It is He who made the sun a radiance and the moon a light, and determined phases for it, so that you may know the number of years and the reckoning.”

And He says:

“They ask you about the new moons. Say, ‘They are markers of time for people and for Hajj.’”

This is why some ordinary people, upon seeing an astrologer correctly predict a future eclipse, suppose that his predictions about other events are of the same kind. This is ignorance, for the first prediction is comparable to his saying that the new crescent will appear either on the thirtieth night or on the thirty-first night. This follows a regular course established by Allah that never fails. It is also comparable to his saying that the sun will set at the end of the day, and similar statements. Whoever knows the positions of the sun and moon and their courses knows this, although such knowledge is of little benefit.

---

## Page 16619

Thus, the fact that an eclipse has an appointed time does not preclude Allah, when that time comes, from making it a cause of whatever He decrees—punishment or otherwise—for those He punishes at that time, or for others upon whom He brings such things. Likewise, Allah’s punishment of people with a fierce, cold wind, as with the people of ‘Ād, occurred at the appropriate time: the end of winter, as scholars of Quranic commentary and accounts of the prophets have mentioned. It is reported of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace:

“Whenever he saw a rain cloud—a cloud thought likely to bring rain—he would pace back and forth, and his expression would change. ‘Ā’ishah said to him, ‘People rejoice when they see a rain cloud!’ He replied, ‘O ‘Ā’ishah, what assurance do I have? The people of ‘Ād saw the punishment as a cloud approaching their valleys and said, “This is a cloud bringing us rain.” Allah Most High said: “Rather, it is what you sought to hasten: a wind containing a painful punishment.”’”

Likewise, the times when Allah sends down mercy—such as the last ten days of Ramadan, the first ten days of Dhū al-Hijjah, the depths of the night, and other such times—are fixed times that neither come early nor fall late. During them, mercy descends that does not descend at other times.

Some versions of the hadiths concerning eclipses contain the Prophet’s words, as reported by Ibn Mājah and others:

“Neither is eclipsed because of anyone’s death or life. Rather, when Allah manifests Himself to anything in His creation, it humbles itself before Him.”

Abū Hāmid and others like him criticised this hadith and rejected it, not on the basis of hadith scholarship—for they have little knowledge of it, as Abū Hāmid used to say of himself: “My knowledge of the science of hadith is meagre”

---

## Page 16620

—but because they believed that if the cause of an eclipse were, for example, the moon blocking the sun’s light from reaching the earth when aligned with it, then it could not be attributed to divine manifestation. Yet the manifestation mentioned does not contradict this cause. For the sun and moon are humbled before Allah at this time when their light is interrupted, its influence upon the earth is suspended, and a barrier is placed between them and the realm of their sway, where their light spreads and takes effect. A king exercising authority over a distant place would likewise be humbled if prevented from doing so.

As for Allah Most High’s words:

“And those who arrange affairs,”

those who arrange affairs are the angels.
