As for Allah’s swearing by the stars, as in His words:

“So I swear by the receding stars, which run their courses and disappear,”

this is like His swearing by other created things, such as the night, the day, the sun, the moon, and others. It signifies the greatness of that by which He swears and draws attention to the signs, lessons, benefits for people, and blessings bestowed upon them that it contains, and other such things. It does not mean that hearts should become attached to it, or that it should be thought to bring good or ill fortune—just as this is not supposed of the things mentioned in:

“By the night as it envelops, and the day as it shines forth,”

or:

“By those that scatter far and wide, and those that bear a burden,”

or:

“By the Mount, and a Book inscribed,”

and similar passages.

The belief that one of the seven heavenly bodies is responsible for a person’s good and ill fortune is a corrupt belief. Anyone who believes that it governs his affairs is a disbeliever. Likewise, if he also invokes it and seeks its aid, that constitutes disbelief and outright polytheism. And the most that

---

## Page 16621

anyone making such a claim can do is base it on the child’s having been born under this ascendant. This alone cannot possibly be the sole influence on the circumstances of this child; at most, it could constitute a very small part of the totality of causes. That much does not entail what has been claimed. Indeed, even factors whose influence on the child is actually known—such as the circumstances of the parents and of the country in which he lives—have an observable effect on his circumstances, yet do not act independently.

Furthermore, it has been said that the early polytheistic Sabian astrologers and their followers would determine a child’s ascendant at birth and give the child a name indicating it. When he grew older and was asked his name, the questioner could infer the configuration of his ascendant. Then these fortune-tellers came along, asking a man his name and his mother’s name, claiming that they could thereby obtain indications of his circumstances. These are layers of darkness, one upon another, contrary to both reason and religion.

As for their selection of auspicious times—determining the ascendant for actions they intend to undertake, such as choosing to travel when the moon is in its exaltation, namely “Cancer,” and not in its fall, namely “Scorpio”—this belongs to the same blameworthy category.

When ‘Alī ibn Abī Tālib intended to set out to fight the Khārijites, an astrologer approached him and said: “O Commander of the Faithful, do not set out, for the moon is in Scorpio. If you set out

---

## Page 16622

while the moon is in Scorpio, your companions will be defeated”—or words to that effect.

ʿAlī replied: “Rather, I shall set out, trusting in Allah, relying upon Him, and rejecting your claim.” He set out, and Allah blessed that expedition, in which he ultimately killed most of the Khārijites. This was among his greatest sources of joy, for he had fought them at the command of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace.

As for the report some people mention that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said, “Do not travel while the moon is in Scorpio,” it is a fabricated lie, according to the consensus of hadith scholars.

As for the claim that this was the art of Idrīs, the first response is: this is an assertion made without knowledge. Such a thing can only be known through an authentic report, and the person making this claim has no means of establishing one. Their books, however, mention “Hermes of Hermeses,” whom they claim to be Idrīs. Among them, “Hermes” is a generic designation; hence their expression “Hermes of Hermeses.” The believer knows with certainty that what they transmit from their Hermes has not been faithfully transmitted from any prophet, because of the lies and falsehood it contains.

The second response is: if this originally came from Idrīs, then it was a miracle granted to him and knowledge Allah bestowed upon him, and would therefore belong to the prophetic sciences. These people, however, appeal only to experience and analogy, not to reports from the prophets, blessings and peace be upon them.

---

## Page 16623

The third response is: even if some of this was taken from a prophet, it is known with certainty that the lies and falsehood it contains far outweigh whatever was taken from that prophet.

It is also known with certainty that the lies and falsehood it contains are many times greater than those found in what the Jews and Christians transmit from the prophets. Now, if we know with absolute certainty that the religion of the Jews and Christians originally came from the messengers, that Allah revealed the Torah, the Gospel, and the Psalms just as He revealed the Quran, and that Allah has commanded us to believe in what He revealed to us and what He revealed to those before us—as He, Most High, says:

“Say, ‘We believe in Allah and in what has been revealed to us, and what was revealed to Abraham, Ishmael, Isaac, Jacob, and the Tribes, and what Moses and Jesus were given, and what the prophets were given by their Lord. We make no distinction between any of them, and to Him we submit’”—

and yet Allah has told us that the People of the Book distorted, altered, lied, and concealed; if this is the condition of revelation whose authenticity is established and scriptures whose divine origin is certain—even though they are much closer to our time than Idrīs, and their transmitters are more eminent than the transmitters of astrology, less prone to deliberate lies and falsehood, and further removed from disbelief in Allah, His Messenger, and the Last Day—then what should we think of this material, if any of it was transmitted from Idrīs? We know that it contains more lies, falsehood, and distortion than the learning of the People of the Book.

It is authentically reported in Sahīh al-Bukhārī that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“When the People of the Book tell you something, neither believe them nor disbelieve them, but say:

---

## Page 16624

‘We believe in Allah and in what has been revealed to us and what has been revealed to you. Our God and your God is One, and to Him we submit.’”

If, concerning what the People of the Book tell us, we are commanded to believe only what we know to be true, just as we are to reject only what we know to be false, how can it be permissible to believe these people concerning what they claim was transmitted from Idrīs, peace be upon him, when they are even further removed from reliable knowledge in this matter than the People of the Book?

A fourth response is: the study of the stars undoubtedly has two branches: calculation and astrological judgments. Calculation concerns knowledge of the dimensions and properties of the celestial spheres and heavenly bodies, the measurements of their movements, and related matters. In principle, this is undoubtedly sound knowledge, like knowledge of the earth and its properties and similar subjects. Yet most of its minute calculations involve much toil and little benefit—as with someone who knows, for example, the precise minutes, seconds, and thirds in the movements of the seven wandering celestial bodies, described as “those that recede,” “which run their courses and disappear.” If the foundations of this knowledge were taken from Idrīs, that is possible; Allah knows best the truth of the matter. Similarly, some people say that the foundations of medicine were taken from one of the prophets.

As for astrological judgments that belong to the category of sorcery, it is impossible that any prophet should have been a sorcerer. These people describe various kinds of sorcery and say, “This is suitable for instituting codes”—meaning “laws and established practices.” Some of these involve invoking the heavenly bodies and worshipping them, and various forms of associating partners with Allah. Everyone who believes in Allah and His Messenger knows as a self-evident certainty that no prophet would command such things

---

## Page 16625

or teach them. Attributing such things to any prophet is like attributing them to Solomon, peace be upon him, when Allah subjected the jinn, human beings, and birds to him. Some people claimed that this was accomplished through various forms of sorcery, to the point that certain groups of Jews and Christians do not regard him as a wise prophet. Allah therefore cleared him of this, saying, Exalted is He:

“They followed what the devils recited during Solomon’s reign. Solomon did not disbelieve, but the devils disbelieved, teaching people sorcery”—to the end of the verse.

Likewise, drawing inferences about events from the celestial movements upon which they rely, and selecting times for undertakings: it is known with certainty that no prophet was ever commanded to do any of this. It contains falsehood and absurdity beneath even rational people who are far below the prophets. Whatever truth it contains is like what their leading authority and Second Teacher, Abū Nasr al-Fārābī, described. He said, in substance:

“If you reversed the astrologers’ classifications, making the auspicious inauspicious and the inauspicious auspicious, the hot cold and the cold hot, the masculine feminine and the feminine masculine, and then made your predictions accordingly, your predictions would be of the same kind as theirs: sometimes right and sometimes wrong.”

They consider Hippocrates, Plato, Aristotle, and his followers among the Peripatetic philosophers above anything of this kind, even though their writings contain falsehood and error comparable to what is found in the writings of the Jews and Christians. If they consider these Sabians and their prophets—who are of lower standing and further removed from knowledge of the truth than the Jews and Christians—above such things, how can it be permissible to attribute them to a noble prophet?

---

## Page 16626

And we know from the lives of our imams that things of this kind have been attributed to Ja‘far al-Sādiq—who was not a prophet—which anyone familiar with Ja‘far, may Allah be pleased with him, knows to be falsely ascribed to him. Indeed, the lies attributed to him are among the most egregious. He has even been credited with prognostications based on “terrestrial movements,” such as the twitching of bodily parts, and atmospheric phenomena such as thunder, lightning, halos, and Allah’s bow, which is called “Qaws Quzah” (the rainbow), and similar things. Scholars know that he is entirely innocent of all this.

Likewise, “the Table,” upon which a group of the Rāfidah based their errors, has been attributed to him. It is a fabrication falsely ascribed to him by Abd Allāh ibn Mu‘āwiya, a man notorious for lying, notwithstanding his leadership and high standing among his followers. The book “Al-Jafr wa-l-Bitāqa wa-l-Haft” has likewise been attributed to him. All of this is falsely ascribed to him, by the agreement of scholars familiar with him.

Even the “Epistles of the Brethren of Purity” have been attributed to him. This displays the utmost ignorance, for these epistles were composed more than two hundred years after his death. He died in 148 AH, whereas these epistles were composed under the Buyid dynasty during the fourth century AH, early in the reign of the Banū Ubayd, who built Cairo. A group composed them, claiming to have reconciled Islamic law and philosophy through them; thus they went astray and led others astray.

The students of Ja‘far al-Sādiq who learned from him—such as Mālik ibn Anas, Sufyān ibn ‘Uyayna, and other imams of Islam like them—are innocent of these fabrications.

---

## Page 16627

Likewise, much of what Shaykh Abū ‘Abd al-Rahmān al-Sulamī attributes to Ja‘far in his “Haqā’iq al-Tafsīr” (Realities of Quranic Exegesis) consists of fabrications whose falsehood no one knowledgeable in this matter doubts. The same applies to many of the false doctrines that the Rāfidah attribute to him: these are among the most blatant lies told about him. Since their emergence, no sect within this community has surpassed the Rāfidah in lying and discord.

The first to introduce Rāfidī doctrine was a hypocrite and heretic called ‘Abd Allāh ibn Saba’. His purpose was to corrupt the Muslims’ religion, just as Paul, the author of the epistles in the Christians’ possession, had done. Paul introduced innovations that corrupted their religion. He had been a Jew, then hypocritically professed Christianity, intending to corrupt it. Ibn Saba’ likewise had been a Jew and pursued the same purpose, fomenting strife in an effort to corrupt the faith. He did not succeed in that; nevertheless, hostility and turmoil arose among the believers, during which ‘Uthmān, may Allah be pleased with him, was killed, and the ensuing strife unfolded as it did.

Yet Allah has not united this community upon error—all praise belongs to Allah. Rather, there will always remain within it a group upholding the truth, unharmed by those who oppose or abandon them, until the Hour comes. This is attested by the widely transmitted reports from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, in the authentic hadith collections.

When the Shiite innovations arose during the caliphate of the Commander of the Faithful, ‘Alī ibn Abī Tālib, may Allah be pleased with him, he rejected them. Their adherents formed “three groups”: extremists, revilers, and those who maintained his superiority.

---

## Page 16628

As for the “extremists,” he burned them with fire. One day, he came out through the Gate of Kindah, and some people prostrated before him. He asked, “What is this?” They replied, “You are Allah Himself.” He called on them three times to repent, but they would not recant. On the third occasion, he ordered trenches to be dug, kindled fires in them, and then cast the men into them. He said:

“When I saw how abhorrent the matter was,
I kindled my fire and called Qanbar.”

Sahīh al-Bukhārī relates that ‘Alī was brought some of their heretics and burned them. News of this reached {Ibn ‘Abbās, who said: “Had it been me, I would not have burned them, because the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, forbade inflicting Allah’s punishment. Instead, I would have struck off their heads, because the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said, ‘Whoever changes his religion, kill him.’”}

As for the “revilers,” when he learned of a man who reviled Abū Bakr and ‘Umar, he sought to put him to death, but the man fled from him to Qarqīsiyā. Someone spoke to him on the man’s behalf. ‘Alī used to conciliate his commanders because he lacked firm control, and they did not obey everything he commanded them to do.

As for “those who maintained his superiority,” he said: “No one who considers me superior to Abū Bakr and ‘Umar will be brought before me without my flogging him with the prescribed punishment for false accusers.” It is narrated from him through more than eighty chains of transmission that he said: “The best of this community after its Prophet is Abū Bakr, then ‘Umar.”

Sahīh al-Bukhārī relates that Muhammad ibn al-Hanafiyyah said to his father: “Father, who is the best of people after the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace?”

---

## Page 16629

He replied, “My son, do you not know?” “No,” he answered. “Abū Bakr,” his father said. “Then who?” he asked. “‘Umar,” he replied. Al-Tirmidhī and others relate that ‘Alī narrated this ranking in merit from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace.

The point here is that lies of various kinds have been fabricated about ‘Alī ibn Abī Tālib—things that could not rightly be attributed even to the least of the believers. Indeed, the Qarmatians, Bātiniyyah, Khurramiyyah, Mazdakites, Ismā‘īlīs, and Nusayrīs attributed their doctrines to him, although these are among the most corrupt doctrines in the world. They claimed that these belonged to the knowledge inherited from him.

All of this was introduced by hypocritical heretics who intended to display the beliefs of the believers while inwardly holding the opposite. They gained the allegiance of groups that had abandoned the revealed laws. They had states of their own, and the believers suffered tribulations at their hands. Ibn Sīnā even said, “I took up the philosophical sciences only because my father was an adherent of the Egyptian mission.” He meant the Banū ‘Ubayd, the Rāfidī Qarmatians, for they espoused these philosophical sciences.

Thus, you find connections and alliances between these people and the Rāfidīs and others like them in their remoteness from understanding prophethood. What unites them is utter ignorance of the straight path—the path of those whom Allah has blessed: the prophets, the steadfastly truthful, the martyrs, and the righteous.

If, then, within a period of less than seven hundred years, lies have been fabricated about his household, his Companions, and others, and doctrines of philosophers and astrologers have been attributed to them which are known

---

## Page 16630

by every rational person to be falsely attributed to them, and these have gained currency among many groups claiming affiliation with this faith—even though there are people who expose these fabricators’ lies, prohibit such things, and defend the faith with their hearts, hands, and tongues—then what should be thought of the astrological and philosophical matters attributed to “Idrīs” and other prophets? Consider the long passage of time, the variety of events, the changes in rule, religious communities, and religions, the absence of anyone to establish the truth of these matters through evidence and proof, and the countless lies and calumnies they contain.

Likewise, the claim that the star of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, was associated with Scorpio and Mars, and that of his community with Venus, and similar assertions, are among the most obvious nonsense. The circumstances of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, contradict what they claim these astrological judgments imply. Among the clearest lies is their assertion that the Muslims’ star is Venus and the Christians’ star is Jupiter, while also saying that Jupiter signifies knowledge and religion, whereas Venus signifies amusement and play. Every rational person knows that Christians are the most ignorant and misguided of religious communities, the furthest from understanding either rational knowledge or transmitted teachings, and the most occupied with amusements and with making them acts of worship.

The philosophers all agree that no greater religious law has ever come into the world than the law brought by Muhammad, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. His community is the most accomplished in intellect, religion, and knowledge, by the agreement of the philosophers—even Jewish and Christian philosophers, who have no doubt that Muslims are superior in intellect and religion.

---

## Page 16631

And any one of them remains in his religion only because he follows his desires and safeguards what he considers his worldly interests, or because he supposes that it is permissible to adhere to any religion whatsoever and that religions resemble the schools within Islam. Most philosophers, astrologers, and others like them hold this view, treating religions like sound political orders, even though some are better than others.

The divinely revealed scriptures transmitted from the prophets, may blessings and peace be upon them, through mass transmission, however, expressly declare that Allah accepts no religion from anyone other than the upright faith. This is Islam in its general sense: worshipping Allah alone, without any partner, and believing in His Books, His messengers, and the Last Day. As the Most High says:

“Indeed, those who believe, the Jews, the Christians, and the Sabians—whoever believes in Allah and the Last Day and does righteous deeds—will have their reward with their Lord. They will have nothing to fear, nor will they grieve.”

He has told us the same concerning the earlier prophets and their communities. Noah said:

“But if you turn away, I have asked you for no reward. My reward rests only with Allah, and I have been commanded to be among the Muslims.”

He said concerning Abraham:

“Who would turn away from the religion of Abraham except one who makes a fool of himself? We certainly chose him in this world, and in the Hereafter he will surely be among the righteous.”

“When his Lord said to him, ‘Submit,’ he said, ‘I have submitted to the Lord of all worlds.’”

“Abraham enjoined this upon his sons, as did Jacob: ‘My sons, Allah has chosen this religion for you, so do not die except as Muslims.’”

Moses said:

“My people, if you have believed in Allah, then put your trust in Him, if you are Muslims.”

And He said:

“Indeed, We sent down the Torah, containing guidance and light, by which the prophets who submitted to Allah judged for the Jews.”

Bilqīs said:

“My Lord, I have indeed wronged myself, and I submit with Solomon to Allah, the Lord of all worlds.”

And He said concerning

---

## Page 16632

the disciples:

“‘Believe in Me and in My messenger.’ They said, ‘We believe; bear witness that we are Muslims.’”

He has also said without qualification:

“Allah bears witness that there is no deity except Him, as do the angels and those endowed with knowledge; He upholds justice. There is no deity except Him, the Almighty, the All-Wise.”

“Indeed, the religion with Allah is Islam.”

And He said:

“Say, ‘We believe in Allah and what has been revealed to us, and what was revealed to Abraham, Ishmael, Isaac, Jacob, and the Tribes, and what Moses, Jesus, and the prophets were given by their Lord. We make no distinction between any of them, and to Him we submit.’”

“Whoever seeks a religion other than Islam, it will never be accepted from him, and in the Hereafter he will be among the losers.”

If Muslims are, as every rational person agrees, foremost among the adherents of religions in knowledge, reason, justice, and similar qualities—which, in their view, correspond to the influences of Jupiter—while Christians are further removed from these qualities and more given to diversion, play, and what they regard as corresponding to the influences of Venus, then what they have claimed is plainly false.

Thus their predictions remain false and incoherent. Indeed, even the leading philosopher whom they call “the philosopher of Islam,” Ya‘qūb ibn Ishāq al-Kindī, made an astrological calculation for this religion, claiming that it would come to an end in the year 693. Someone then borrowed this from him and presented it as a “derivation” from the letters of words that had appeared through mystical unveiling to one of those who repeated it. They were also supported in this by someone who claimed to have calculated how long this religion would last from the numerical values of the letters found in

---

## Page 16633

the openings of the surahs. With repetitions omitted, these comprise fourteen letters. Their total value in the greater abjad reckoning is six hundred and ninety-three. Another example is the report mentioned in Quranic commentary that, when Allah revealed “Alif Lām Mīm,” some Jews said, “This religion will endure for thirty-one years.” Then, when He subsequently revealed “Alif Lām Rā” and “Alif Lām Mīm,” they said, “He has confused matters for us.”

These practices, found among the errors of the Jews and Christians and those of the polytheists and Sabians among the philosophizers and astrologers, contain an extent of such falsehood known only to Allah Most High. These practices and others like them lie outside the religion of Islam and are forbidden within it. It is therefore incumbent upon Muslims—everyone who is able—to denounce and forbid them through knowledge and clear explanation, by deed and word. This is among the greatest duties Allah has imposed concerning enjoining what is right and forbidding what is wrong. Such people and their like are enemies of the messengers and a rot within religions.

Falsehood gains currency in the world only when mixed with some truth. Thus, the People of the Book have mixed truth with falsehood: through the little truth they possess, they lead many people away from the truth in which they must believe and invite them to the abundant falsehood they themselves follow. Often, those Muslims who oppose them cannot properly distinguish truth from falsehood, present an argument that refutes their falsehood, or explain Allah’s proof, which He established through His messengers. This gives rise to confusion and trial. We have discussed this falsehood and similar matters at length elsewhere. And Allah knows best.

---

## Page 16634

He—may Allah Most High have mercy on him—was asked:

What do the esteemed jurists, the imams of religion—may Allah be pleased with them all—say concerning these “astrologers” who sit along the roads, in shops, and elsewhere? Women sit with them, as do immoral men drawn there by the women. These astrologers claim to receive information about hidden matters through the practice of astrology. They write magic squares for people, practise sorcery, write talismans, and teach women sorcery to use on their husbands and others. Consequently, women and men gather at the shop entrances. This may lead to further consequences: turning wives against their husbands, corrupting people’s beliefs, and the ignorant among them becoming attached to sorcery and the stars and turning away from Allah, Mighty and Majestic, and from reliance upon Him when events and calamities befall them.

Is this lawful or not? Is the practice of “astrology” forbidden or not? Is it permissible to accept payment for this, and is making such payments forbidden or not? May anyone responsible for the shop—whether its administrator, owner, or agent—rent it out for such purposes or not? Is the rent forbidden or not? Must the ruler and every Muslim capable of doing so put an end to these practices or not?

---

## Page 16635

And if the ruler fails to act against them, does he fall under the warning in the authentic hadith reported from the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—in which he said:

“No ruler whom Allah entrusts with subjects, but who then fails to strive for their welfare and sincerely advise them, will enter Paradise with them.”

If the ruler acts against this wrongdoing, does he come within the words of Allah Most High:

“Let there be among you a group inviting to good, enjoining what is right and forbidding what is wrong. It is they who will be successful”?

Will he receive a great reward for opposing it or not? Would the esteemed scholars also kindly mention whatever hadiths containing warnings on this matter come to mind? May they be rewarded, Allah Most High willing.

He replied:

Praise be to Allah, Lord of all worlds. None of these practices is lawful. The practice of “astrology,” concerned with predictions and celestial influence—that is, inferring earthly events from celestial conditions and combining celestial forces with their earthly recipients—is forbidden by the Book, the Sunnah, and the consensus of the Muslim community. Indeed, it has been forbidden through the teachings of all the messengers in every revealed religion. Allah Most High says:

“The magician will never succeed, wherever he may go.”

He also says:

“Have you not seen those who were given a share of the Scripture, yet believe in jibt and tāghūt (false objects of worship)?”

‘Umar and others said: “Jibt means sorcery.”

Abū Dāwūd narrated in his Sunan, with a sound chain of transmission, from Qabīsa ibn Mukhāriq that the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—said:

“Divination from birds, divination by tracing lines, and belief in omens are forms of sorcery.”

‘Awf said:

---

## Page 16636

the narrator of the hadith:

“‘Iyāfa is drawing omens by making birds fly, while tarq is drawing lines on the ground.”

It has also been said that the definitions are the other way round. If drawing lines and similar practices, which are branches of astrology, are forms of sorcery, then what of astrology itself? This is because they generate figures on the ground, these being derived from the configurations of the heavens.

Ahmad, Abū Dāwūd, Ibn Mājah and others narrated with an authentic chain of transmission from Ibn ‘Abbās, who said that the Messenger of Allah—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—said:

“Whoever acquires knowledge of astrology acquires a branch of sorcery; the more he learns, the more sorcery he acquires.”

The Messenger of Allah—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—thus explicitly stated that astrology is a form of sorcery. Allah Most High has said:

“The magician will never succeed, wherever he may go.”

Reality bears this out: observation shows that astrologers do not prosper, either in this world or in the Hereafter.

Ahmad narrated, as did Muslim in his Sahīh, from Safiyya bint ‘Ubayd, from one of the wives of the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—that the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—said:

“Whoever visits a fortune-teller and asks him about anything will have no prayer accepted from him for forty days.”

According to some scholars, the term “fortune-teller” includes the astrologer; according to others, he falls under the same ruling. If this is the condition of the questioner, what, then, of the one questioned?

Muslim also narrated in his Sahīh that Mu‘āwiya ibn al-Hakam al-Sulamī said:

“I said, ‘O Messenger of Allah, some of our people visit soothsayers.’ He replied, ‘Then do not visit them.’”

The Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—thus forbade visiting soothsayers. The term “soothsayer” includes the astrologer according to al-Khattābī

---

## Page 16637

and other scholars, and this usage has been reported from the Arabs. Other scholars regard the astrologer as belonging to the same category as the soothsayer, but as being even worse; he therefore comes under the same ruling because the underlying meaning applies to him.

It is narrated in the Sahīh that he—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—said:

“The price of a dog is foul, a prostitute’s earnings are foul, and a soothsayer’s fee is foul.”

His fee is what ordinary people call his “sweetener.” This also covers payments involving astrologers, those who practise divination with arrows or lots—such as a piece of wood inscribed with a, b, j, d—those who divine using pebbles, and others like them. Whatever is given to these people is unlawful.

More than one scholar has reported consensus on its prohibition, including al-Baghawī, al-Qādī ‘Iyād and others.

The two Sahīhs relate from Zayd ibn Khālid, who said:

“The Messenger of Allah—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—addressed us at al-Hudaybiya after rain had fallen during the night. He asked, ‘Do you know what your Lord said last night?’ We replied, ‘Allah and His Messenger know best.’ He said, ‘This morning, some of My servants believe in Me and others disbelieve in Me. Whoever says, “We were given rain by Allah’s grace and mercy,” believes in Me and disbelieves in the stars.’”

In Sahīh Muslim, Abū Hurayra reports that the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—said:

“Whenever Allah sends down a blessing from heaven, some people respond to it with disbelief: Allah sends down rain, and they say, ‘It was through such-and-such a star.’”

Sahīh Muslim also reports that he—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—said:

“Four practices in my community belong to the ways of pre-Islamic ignorance: boasting of ancestral standing, disparaging people’s lineage, wailing over the dead, and seeking rain through the stars.”

It also relates from Ibn ‘Abbās, from the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—concerning the verse, “And do you repay your provision with denial?” that he said:

“This refers to seeking rain through the stars”—or words to that effect.

---

## Page 16638

Reports from the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—his Companions, and the other leading scholars forbidding this are too numerous to cite here. What we have mentioned makes clear that fees, gifts, and gratuities paid for such practices are unlawful for both the giver and the recipient. It is also unlawful for owners, endowment administrators, and agents to rent privately owned or endowed shops, or other premises, to these disbelievers and evildoers for this purpose, if they believe it likely that they will practise this accursed occult art there.

The ruler and everyone capable must strive to put an end to this, preventing them from occupying shops or roadsides, or entering people’s homes, for this purpose. If someone fails to do so, these words of the Most High are warning enough:

“They did not forbid one another from the wrongdoing they committed.”

And His words—glorified and exalted is He:

“Why do their godly scholars and rabbis not forbid them from sinful speech and consuming unlawful gain?”

For these accursed people utter sinful speech and consume unlawful gain, by the consensus of the Muslims. It is also authentically reported from the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—through al-Siddīq that he said:

“When people see wrongdoing and do not change it, Allah may soon encompass them all with punishment from Him.”

What wrongdoing could be more reprehensible than the deeds of these vile people, who gnaw away at the realm, the enemies of the messengers and the offspring of the star-worshipping Sabians? Was not Abraham, Allah’s intimate friend—may Allah’s blessings and peace be upon him—the leader of those devoted to pure monotheism, sent to their predecessors? Nimrod son of Canaan was their king, and the scholars of the Sabians were the astrologers and their like. Has not the worship of idols generally arisen from the counsel of this vile class, who consume people’s wealth unjustly and turn people away from Allah’s path?

---

## Page 16639

And anyone whose support they enlist among those who profess to follow a revealed scripture is indeed liable to fall, in some measure, under His words:

“And when a messenger from Allah came to them, confirming what was with them, a group of those who had been given the Scripture cast the Book of Allah behind their backs, as though they did not know.”

“And they followed what the devils recited during the reign of Solomon. Solomon did not disbelieve, but the devils disbelieved, teaching people magic and what was sent down to the two angels in Babylon, Hārūt and Mārūt. Yet neither of them taught anyone without first saying, ‘We are only a trial, so do not disbelieve.’ They learned from them that by which they caused separation between a man and his wife. But they could not harm anyone thereby except by Allah’s permission. They learned what harmed them and did not benefit them. They certainly knew that whoever purchased it would have no share in the Hereafter. Wretched indeed was that for which they sold themselves, if only they knew.”

Likewise, leading astrologers of earlier and later times have acknowledged that Allah averts from believers devoted to worship and supplication—through the blessing of their worship, their supplication, and their reliance upon Allah—what astrologers claim the celestial spheres necessarily bring about. They also acknowledge that those devoted to worship and supplication who rely upon Allah are granted rewards in this world and the Hereafter that the celestial spheres have no power to bring about.

All praise, then, belongs to Allah, who has placed the good of this world and the Hereafter in following the messengers, and has made those who enjoin what is right and forbid what is wrong the best community. The Most High said:

“Allah will then bring forth a people whom He loves and who love Him, humble toward the believers, mighty against the disbelievers, striving in Allah’s cause and fearing no critic’s blame. That

---

## Page 16640

is Allah’s bounty, which He grants to whom He wills. And Allah is All-Encompassing, All-Knowing.” Allah grants support and assistance in adhering to the religion and following the way of the believers. Allah, glorified and exalted, knows best and is most wise.

He—may Allah Most High have mercy on him—was asked:

“Is the practice of ‘astrology’ and its use to predict events lawful or forbidden? Is receiving or paying fees for it permissible? Must the ruler prevent its practitioners from practising and remove them from their places in shops?”

He replied:

“Rather, this is forbidden by the consensus of the Muslims, as is receiving payment for it. And [preventing them] (1) from setting up in shops and on the streets, preventing people from renting to them, and taking action in this matter are among the finest forms of striving in Allah’s cause. Allah knows best.”

He—may Allah have mercy on him—was asked:

“Concerning someone who said to a sharīf (a man of noble lineage), ‘You dog, you son of a dog! Do not put your hand into the bathhouse pool.’ He was told, ‘He is a sharīf,’ whereupon he said, ‘May Allah curse him and curse whoever ennobled him.’ He was then told, ‘Have you lost your senses? This man is a sharīf!’ He replied, ‘A dog, the son of a dog!’ Then he rose, went up to him, and struck him. Must he be put to death or not, when the person who testified against him concerning this was an enemy of his?”

> **Footnotes:**
> (1) The text in square brackets is absent from the printed edition, and I did not find it in Siyānat Majmū‘ al-Fatāwā min al-Saqt wa-l-Tashīf (Safeguarding the Collected Fatwas from Omissions and Scribal Errors).
> Usāma ibn al-Zahrā’ — book coordinator for al-Mawsū‘a al-Shāmila.

---

## Page 16641

He replied:

An enemy’s testimony against his enemy is not accepted, even if he is otherwise upright. These words, by themselves, do not constitute the kind of insult for which the speaker is put to death. Rather, he must be asked what he meant by “whoever ennobled him.” If his explanation, or circumstantial or verbal indications, establish that he intended to curse the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, he must be put to death.

If this is not established, or if circumstantial or verbal indications establish that he meant someone other than the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace—for example, someone who honours or reveres that man, or considers him a man of noble lineage—then, by scholarly consensus, this does not warrant execution. Someone who is not a zindīq is not presumed to intend to curse the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. Thus, when someone is known to be a believer and not a zindīq, that is evidence that he did not mean the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. By scholarly consensus, a Muslim must not be put to death for insulting someone of noble lineage. Execution is prescribed for insulting the prophets. As for insulting the Companions, scholars make distinctions and differ concerning the ruling.

However, anyone proven to have wronged a person of noble lineage or anyone else, whether verbally or physically, is punished for that wrongdoing: through equivalent retaliation, a sanction that deters further wrongdoing, or the prescribed punishment for slander if the offence constitutes slander warranting that punishment.

Wrongdoers must also be punished even if they themselves are of noble lineage. It is authentically reported in the two Sahīh collections that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Those before you were ruined because, when someone of high status among them stole, they let him go, but when someone weak among them stole, they inflicted the prescribed punishment upon him. By the One who

---

## Page 16642

holds Muhammad’s soul in His hand, if Fātimah, Muhammad’s daughter, were to steal, I would cut off her hand.”

Where equivalent retaliation is prescribed in matters of bloodshed, property, or otherwise, no distinction is made between a person of noble lineage and anyone else. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Muslims’ lives are of equal worth, and a pledge of protection given by the least of them is binding upon them all”—to the end of the hadith.

And Allah knows best.

He—may Allah Most High have mercy on him—was asked:

A man intended to lodge a complaint against another man. A group interceded on the latter’s behalf, but he said, “Even if Muhammad ibn Abd Allāh came to me on his behalf, I would not accept his intercession.”

They said, “You have committed disbelief! Ask Allah to forgive you for what you have said.” He replied, “What should I say?”

He—may Allah Most High have mercy on him—answered:

As for the man’s words, “Even if Muhammad ibn Abd Allāh came to me”: if it is established that he said this, he is to be put to death for it. Even if he repents after his case has been brought before the ruler, this does not spare him execution, according to the stronger of the two scholarly views. However, if he repents before his case is brought before the ruler, he is spared execution, according to the stronger of the two views. If he is subjected to discretionary punishment after repentance, that is permissible.

---

## Page 16643

He—may Allah have mercy on him—was asked:

A man cursed the Jews, cursed his religion, and reviled the Torah. Is it permissible for a Muslim to revile their scripture or not?

He answered:

Praise be to Allah. No one may curse the Torah. Indeed, anyone who curses the Torah without qualification must be called upon to repent; if he does not repent, he is to be put to death. If he knows that it was revealed by Allah and that belief in it is obligatory, he is to be put to death for reviling it, and his repentance is not accepted according to the stronger of the two scholarly views.

But if he curses the religion that the Jews practise in this age, there is nothing wrong with his doing so, for they and their religion are accursed. Likewise, if he reviles the Torah in their possession in terms that make clear that he intends to speak of its distortion—for example, by saying, “The copies of this Torah have been altered, and it is not permissible to act upon their contents. Anyone who today follows its altered and abrogated laws is a disbeliever”—then such statements and the like are true, and no blame attaches to the one who says them. And Allah knows best.

---

## Page 16644

He—may Allah Most High have mercy on him—was asked:

About a man who considers the Jews and Christians superior to the Rāfidah?

He answered:

Praise be to Allah. Everyone who believes in what Muhammad—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—brought is better than anyone who disbelieves in him, even if that believer adheres to some form of religious innovation, whether that of the Khawārij, Shia, Murji’ah, Qadariyyah, or others. For the disbelief of the Jews and Christians is a matter necessarily known in Islam. If someone who adheres to religious innovation believes that he is following the Messenger—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—and not opposing him, he is not thereby a disbeliever in him. Even supposing that he does fall into disbelief, his disbelief is not like that of someone who declares the Messenger—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—to be a liar.

He—may Allah Most High have mercy on him—was also asked:

About a man who said:

The Messenger of Allah—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—said:

“Whoever says, ‘There is no god but Allah,’ will enter Paradise.”

Another man said: “If he follows the praiseworthy path and adheres to the revealed law, he is included within the scope of this hadith. But if he acts otherwise and does not care what he loses of his religion

---

## Page 16645

and gains in his worldly life, he is not included within the scope of this hadith.”

The man who quoted the hadith said to him: “If I were to do everything unbecoming and say, ‘There is no god but Allah,’ would I enter Paradise without entering the Fire?”

He—may Allah have mercy on him—answered:

Praise be to Allah, Lord of all worlds. Whoever believes that merely uttering these words ensures that a person will enter Paradise and never enter the Fire under any circumstances is astray, contradicting the Quran, the Sunnah, and the consensus of the believers. For the hypocrites, who will be in the lowest depths of the Fire, have uttered these words, and they are many. Indeed, hypocrites may fast, pray, and give charity, yet none of this is accepted from them. Allah Most High says:

“Indeed, the hypocrites seek to deceive Allah, but He deceives them. When they stand for prayer, they stand lazily, showing off before people, and they remember Allah only a little.”

And He Most High says:

“Say, ‘Spend willingly or unwillingly; it will never be accepted from you. Indeed, you are a defiantly disobedient people.’”

“Nothing prevents their contributions from being accepted except that they disbelieve in Allah and His Messenger, come to prayer only lazily, and spend only reluctantly.”

And He Most High says:

“Indeed, Allah will gather the hypocrites and the disbelievers all together in Hell.”

And He Most High says:

“On the Day when Allah will not disgrace the Prophet and those who believe with him. Their light will shine ahead of them and on their right. They will say, ‘Our Lord, perfect our light for us.’”

And He Most High says:

“On the Day you see the believing men and women with their light shining ahead of them and on their right: ‘Good news for you today: Gardens beneath which rivers flow.’”

—up to His words—

“So today no ransom will be accepted from you, nor from those who disbelieved.”

In the two Sahīh collections, it is reported that the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—said:

“The signs of a hypocrite are three: when he speaks, he lies; when he makes a promise, he breaks it; and when he is entrusted with something, he betrays that trust.”

Muslim’s version adds: “Even if

---

## Page 16646

he prays and fasts and claims to be a Muslim.”

In the two Sahīh collections, it is also reported that he—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—said:

“Whoever possesses four traits is a complete hypocrite, and whoever possesses one of them has a trait of hypocrisy until he abandons it: when he speaks, he lies; when he makes a promise, he breaks it; when he makes a covenant, he betrays it; and when he disputes, he behaves wickedly.”

However, if a person says, “There is no god but Allah,” sincerely and truthfully from his heart, and dies upon that, he will not remain in the Fire forever. No one with a mustard seed’s weight of faith in his heart will remain in the Fire forever, as established in authentic hadiths from the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace.

But those who enter it from among “the sinful among those who face the qiblah in prayer”—those who commit theft, unlawful sexual intercourse, drink wine, bear false witness, consume usury, devour orphans’ wealth, and others—if they are punished in it, Allah will punish them in proportion to their sins. As stated in the authentic hadiths:

“For some, the Fire will reach their ankles; for others, their knees; and for others, their waists. After remaining there for as long as Allah wills, they will be brought out like charcoal. They will then be cast into a river called Life, and will grow again in it as a seed grows in the debris carried by a flood. They will enter Paradise with these words inscribed upon their necks: ‘These are those who were in Hell, whom Allah has freed from the Fire.’”

This matter is discussed in detail elsewhere. And Allah knows best.

---

## Page 16647

He—may Allah Most High have mercy on him—was also asked:

About a man who, in accordance with Islamic law, had a debtor imprisoned. Another man came to intercede on the debtor’s behalf, but he refused his intercession. The two disputed over this, and the intercessor testified against the creditor because he had uttered words amounting to disbelief. The creditor feared the consequences and was brought before a Shāfiʿī judge. A Muslim brought a claim against him, alleging that he had uttered the words attributed to him, and requested a ruling under Islamic law.

The judge questioned the defendant about this, but he did not admit it. He was then prompted to confess so that a ruling affirming his Islam and the inviolability of his life could be completed. He admitted that he had uttered those words, unaware of their consequences. He then embraced Islam, pronounced the two testimonies of faith, repented, and sought Allah Most High’s forgiveness. He then asked the aforementioned judge to rule that he was a Muslim, that his life was inviolable, that his repentance was accepted, and that his property remained his. The judge granted his request, ruling that he was a Muslim, that his life was inviolable, that his property remained his, and that his repentance was accepted. He also imposed a discretionary punishment appropriate to his case, ruled that he was not liable to a second discretionary punishment, and adjudicated accordingly in all these matters.

Another judge, a Hanafī, then gave effect to this ruling.

Is the aforementioned judgment valid in every matter decided in his favour? Does the Shāfiʿī judge’s ruling require the presence of an opposing party representing the public treasury? May anyone, on account of what he uttered, seek to take his property or any part of it after his embracing Islam? And is it lawful for another judge, after the judgment

---

## Page 16648

and enforcement mentioned above, to issue a ruling concerning his property that contradicts the initial judgment and its enforcement? Will the ruler be rewarded for preventing anyone from taking his property, or any part of it, on the grounds mentioned?

He replied:

Praise be to Allah. Yes, the aforementioned judgment is valid, as is its enforcement. By the agreement of the Muslims, the public treasury has no claim to the property of someone in this situation. A ruling affirming his Islam and the inviolability of his property does not require the presence of an opposing party representing the public treasury, for neither of these depends upon a judicial ruling. The imams agree that when an apostate embraces Islam, his Islam renders his life and property inviolable, even if no judge rules to that effect. The administrator of the public treasury has no claim concerning the property of someone who embraces Islam after apostasy.

Indeed, the position of al-Shāfiʿī and Abū Hanīfah, and also of Ahmad according to the well-known view reported from him, is that if legally valid testimony of apostasy is given against someone, but he denies it and pronounces the two requisite testimonies of faith, he is deemed a Muslim. He need not confess to what the witnesses have alleged against him. How much more so, then, when no upright witness has testified against him? In such a case, by the agreement of the Muslims, a ruling affirming the inviolability of his life and property does not depend upon his confession.

The inviolability of such a person’s life does not require him to confess and then embrace Islam after being driven to do so. This may entail compelling him to lie about himself by saying that he committed disbelief. For this reason, such a confession may not be accorded the legal effect of a valid confession, since it is known that he was prompted to confess and was, in effect, coerced into doing so; for he did so only

---

## Page 16649

out of fear of being killed. Even supposing that the apostate’s disbelief consisted of blasphemous abuse, no judge adhering to the schools of the four imams would rule that his property belongs to the public treasury after he embraces Islam. Those who rule that he should be executed do so only because, according to their well-known position, his execution is a prescribed punishment. Whoever holds that he should be executed for his concealed unbelief also holds that he cannot be held liable on the basis of such a confession.

Moreover, according to most of those who hold that view, the property of someone who conceals his unbelief passes to his Muslim heirs. When the hypocrites who lived during the Prophet’s lifetime—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—died, Muslims inherited from them despite their hypocrisy being known with certainty. Such was the case with ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ubayy and others whose heirs inherited from them while knowing of their hypocrisy. Nor did any Companion and hypocrite inherit from one another otherwise than by inheritance. A hypocrite is what the jurists who discuss the repentance of the zindīq call a zindīq.

Furthermore, if the judge’s ruling takes effect concerning his life, where there may be disagreement, it applies with still greater force to his property. No one in the Muslim community holds that his property may be confiscated while his life remains inviolable. Such a position would contradict consensus. Thus, if a ruling affirming the inviolability of his life does not require a claim brought on behalf of the ruler, this applies all the more to his property.

It has therefore become clear, on several grounds, that the property of someone in this situation cannot be adjudged to belong to the public treasury.

“First”: Nothing rendering his life forfeit has been established against him, whether through legally valid evidence or an unequivocal confession;

---

## Page 16650

but only through a confession intended to secure the inviolability of his property and life, akin to a claim brought against a nominal defendant.

“Second”: Ruling that his life and property are inviolable is obligatory according to al-Shāfiʿī and the majority, even if he makes no confession. Indeed, in the absence of legally valid evidence and a confession, it is obligatory by consensus.

“Third”: The ruling is undoubtedly valid.

“Fourth”: Even if the ruling were open to juristic disagreement, that disagreement would be resolved by its ratification by the enforcing authority.

“Fifth”: No judge would award this man’s property to the public treasury even if his disbelief and subsequent acceptance of Islam were established—even if that disbelief consisted of blasphemous abuse. How, then, could this be done when his disbelief has not been established? And how could it be done when a ruling has already affirmed the inviolability of his property? Indeed, the schools of Mālik and Ahmad, which are invoked in cases such as this, are among those furthest from awarding such a person’s property to the public treasury. This is because, in their view, a confession of this kind is a confession made as a protective expedient, to which no weight is given, and because of their known position concerning one who commits blasphemous abuse. And Allah knows best.

---

## Page 16651

The Book of Foods

Shaykh al-Islām—may Allah sanctify his soul—was asked whether eating horse meat is lawful.

He answered:

Praise be to Allah. It is lawful according to the majority of scholars, including al-Shāfiʿī, Ahmad, the two leading disciples of Abū Hanīfah, and most jurists of the hadith tradition. It is authentically reported in the two Sahīh collections that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace,

“forbade donkey meat in the year of Khaybar and permitted horse meat.”

It is also authentically reported that

“they slaughtered a horse during the lifetime of the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and its meat was eaten.”

Shaykh al-Islām—may Allah have mercy on him—was asked whether a mule born of a wild ass and a horse may be eaten.

He answered:

If a mule is born of a horse and a wild ass, or of a she-ass and a stallion, it is permissible to eat it. The same applies to every animal born of two parents whose meat is lawful. What is forbidden is an animal born of one lawful parent and one unlawful parent, such as a mule one of whose parents is a domestic donkey; a predatory beast born of a hyena and a wolf; or the isbār, born of a wolf and a male hyena. And Allah knows best.

---

## Page 16652

He—may Allah Most High have mercy on him—was asked about a ewe that gave birth to a lamb, half dog and half lamb, with the two halves divided lengthwise: “Is it lawful to eat it, or is the lamb side lawful to eat?”

He answered:

Praise be to Allah. No part of it may be eaten, for it is born of a lawful animal and an unlawful one, even if the parts are distinguishable. It may only be eaten after ritual slaughter, and ritual slaughter is not valid for such an animal because of this intermixture. And Allah knows best.

He—may Allah Most High have mercy on him—was asked about a man’s goat that gave birth to a female kid and then died. His wife breastfed the kid: “Is it permissible to eat its meat or drink its milk?”

He answered:

Praise be to Allah. Yes, he may do so.

---

## Page 16653

He—may Allah have mercy on him—was asked: “Is it permissible to drink ‘al-iqsamā’?”

He answered:

Praise be to Allah. If it is made from raisins alone, the scholars agree that it is permissible to drink it for three days, provided it has not become intoxicating. However, if it is made from two ingredients, one of which causes the other to spoil, such as raisins and unripe dates, or if it has been left for more than three days, then there is disagreement concerning it.

If something is added to make it sour, such as vinegar or the like, or lemon juice—as is done with fuqqā‘ al-mushadhdhab (a barley drink)—then it is permissible to drink it without restriction, for its acidity prevents it from becoming intoxicating. Thus, all these drinks are permissible to drink if they turn sour without becoming intoxicating.

He—may Allah Most High have mercy on him—was asked about a man who stopped among some people, having no food for himself or his mount. The people refused either to sell him food or to offer him hospitality, and both he and his mount suffered harm: “May he take from them enough to meet his needs without their consent?”

---

## Page 16654

He answered:

If he and his mount are in dire need, and the people have food but do not feed him, he may take enough to meet his needs without their consent and pay them the fair market price. If he is travelling, they must offer him hospitality if they are able to do so. If they do not, he may take what is due to him as a guest without their consent, and he owes them nothing.

The Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—said:

“Honouring the guest’s right is obligatory upon every Muslim.”

He also said:

“Whoever stops among a people, they must offer him hospitality. If they do not, he may exact from their crops and property the equivalent of the hospitality due to him.”

And he said:

“Whoever believes in Allah and the Last Day should honour his guest. His special welcome is for one day and one night, and hospitality is for three days. Anything beyond that is charity.”

And Allah knows best.

---

## Page 16655

Chapter: Ritual Slaughter

Shaykh al-Islām—may Allah sanctify his soul—was asked:

About a group of Muslims who vehemently condemn, without qualification, anyone who eats an animal slaughtered by a Jew or a Christian, although it is not known whether those Jews and Christians entered their religion before its abrogation and alteration and before the mission of the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—or afterwards. Yet they intermarry, and everyone recognizes their marriages. They are non-Muslims under Muslim protection who pay the jizya, but neither their identities nor those of their forefathers are known. Do those who object have the right to prevent them from slaughtering animals for Muslims? Or may Muslims eat animals slaughtered by them, as elsewhere in Muslim lands?

He answered—may Allah be pleased with him:

No one has the right to condemn anyone for eating an animal slaughtered by Jews or Christians in this age, nor to declare it unlawful for them to slaughter animals for Muslims. Whoever objects to this is ignorant and mistaken, and acts contrary to the consensus of the Muslims. The underlying issue is, in fact, a matter of well-known disagreement among Muslim scholars. In matters subject to independent legal reasoning, objection is permissible only through presenting evidence and making the correct course clear—not through mere condemnation based on

---

## Page 16656

mere adherence to authority; for this is the conduct of people of ignorance and caprice. This is all the more so since the view that such slaughter is unlawful, whether now or previously, is exceedingly weak and contrary to what is known of the Sunnah of Allah’s Messenger—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—and of the practice of his Companions and those who followed them in righteousness. Anyone who objects to this must hold one of “two views.”

Either he belongs to those who prohibit “animals slaughtered by the People of the Book” without qualification, as some of the Rāfidah maintain. These people prohibit marrying women of the People of the Book and eating animals slaughtered by them. This is not the position of any Muslim imam renowned for issuing fatwas, nor of their followers.

It is an erroneous view that contradicts the Quran, the Sunnah, and the consensus of the early Muslims. For Allah Most High says in His Book:

“The food of those who were given the Scripture is lawful for you, and your food is lawful for them. And [lawful in marriage are] chaste believing women and chaste women from among those who were given the Scripture before you.”

If it is said that this verse conflicts with His words:

“Do not marry polytheistic women until they believe,”

and His words, Most High:

“Do not hold on to marriage bonds with disbelieving women,”

then the answer is threefold. First, the Quran’s unqualified references to polytheism do not include the People of the Book; they are included only in qualified references to polytheism. Allah Most High says:

“Those who disbelieved among the People of

---

## Page 16657

the Scripture and the polytheists.”

He thus distinguished the polytheists as a category separate from the People of the Book. Allah Most High also says:

“Indeed, those who believe, the Jews, the Sabians, the Christians, the Magians, and those who associate partners with Allah.”

He thus made them a separate category from the others.

As for their inclusion in qualified references to polytheism, this occurs in His words, Most High:

“They have taken their rabbis and monks, and the Messiah, son of Mary, as lords besides Allah, though they were commanded only to worship one God. There is no god but Him. Exalted is He above the partners they associate with Him.”

He thus described them as associating partners with Him. The reason for this distinction is that their religion, as originally revealed by Allah in His Books and conveyed by His messengers, contained no polytheism. As Allah Most High says:

“We never sent a messenger before you without revealing to him: ‘There is no god but Me, so worship Me.’”

Allah Most High also says:

“Ask those of Our messengers whom We sent before you: Did We appoint any gods besides the Most Merciful to be worshipped?”

And He says:

“We certainly sent a messenger to every nation, saying: ‘Worship Allah and shun false gods.’”

But they altered and changed their religion, introducing forms of polytheism for which Allah had sent down no authority. Thus, polytheism came to exist among them through what they introduced, not through the original religion.

As for His words, Most High:

“Do not hold on to marriage bonds with disbelieving women,”

the definite expression “disbelieving women” refers to the particular women who were married to Muslims. Those were polytheistic women from Mecca and similar places, not women of the People of the Book.

---

## Page 16658

The second argument

Even if the expressions “polytheistic women” and “disbelieving women” are understood to include women of the People of the Book, the verse in Sūrat al-Mā’idah is specific and was revealed later. It was revealed after Sūrat al-Baqarah and Sūrat al-Mumtahanah, by scholarly consensus, as stated in the hadith:

“Al-Mā’idah was among the last portions of the Quran to be revealed, so regard as lawful what it declares lawful and as forbidden what it declares forbidden.”

The Muslim scholars agree that a later specific text takes precedence over an earlier general text. However, the majority say that it explains the earlier text, making clear that the case excluded by the specific text was never intended by the general wording. Another group says that the ruling concerning that case was abrogated after having been prescribed.

The third argument

If we suppose that both texts are specific, then one text prohibited meat slaughtered by the People of the Book and marriage to their women, while the other permitted both. Here, the text permitting them must be given precedence for two reasons.

The first is that Sūrat al-Mā’idah was revealed later, by scholarly consensus, and therefore abrogates the earlier text. It cannot be argued that this amounts to abrogating the ruling twice, because engaging in those practices before their prohibition was not based on a revealed legal pronouncement permitting them. Rather, it was due to the absence of a prohibition, just as with drinking wine, eating pork, and similar practices.

A newly introduced prohibition does not constitute abrogation of a ruling based on the presumption of continuity. Thus, the Prophet’s prohibition—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—of

“every fanged beast of prey and every bird with talons”

did not abrogate what is indicated by the words of Allah Most High:

“Say: ‘I do not find in what has been revealed to me anything forbidden to one who would eat it’”—and the rest of the verse—

namely, that before this verse was revealed, Allah, Mighty and Majestic, had prohibited only these three categories. For this verse negated the prohibition of anything other than those three up to the time of its revelation, without establishing the lawfulness of

---

## Page 16659

anything else. Rather, anything else was left unregulated, neither expressly permitted nor prohibited, as with the acts of a child or a mentally unsound person. As stated in the well-known hadith:

“Lawful is what Allah has made lawful in His Book, and forbidden is what Allah has forbidden in His Book. Whatever He has remained silent about is among the things He has overlooked.”

This report is preserved from Salmān al-Fārisī, either as his own statement or as a report traced back to the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace.

This is further indicated by Allah’s statement in Sūrat al-Mā’idah:

“Today all good things have been made lawful for you.”

He thus states that He made them lawful that day. Sūrat al-Mā’idah is Medinan by scholarly consensus, while Sūrat al-An‘ām is Meccan by scholarly consensus. It follows that good things were declared lawful in Medina, not Mecca. Allah Most High also says:

“They ask you what has been made lawful for them. Say: ‘All good things have been made lawful for you.’”

And Allah Most High says:

“The food of those who were given the Scripture is lawful for you, and your food is lawful for them”—and the rest of the verse.

Thus, the lawfulness of marriage to women of the People of the Book is established. It has been said that this had either been left unregulated—as is the correct view—or had been forbidden, with that prohibition subsequently abrogated. This is supported by the fact that nothing has abrogated the verse of Sūrat al-Mā’idah.

The second reason

The lawfulness of the food of the People of the Book is established by the Quran, the Sunnah, and scholarly consensus. The discussion concerning marriage to their women is the same as that concerning the meat they slaughter: once the lawfulness of either is established, the lawfulness of the other is established as well. Moreover, there is no evidence whatsoever opposing the lawfulness of their food.

Further evidence is that Hudhayfah ibn al-Yamān married a Jewish woman, and none of the Companions objected. This indicates that they were unanimous in permitting it.

---

## Page 16660

If it is said that Allah Most High’s words,

“The food of those who were given the Scripture is lawful for you,”

refer to fruits and grains, the reply is that this is mistaken for several reasons.

First, these are permissible whether obtained from the People of the Book, polytheists, or Magians, so there would be no point in singling out the People of the Book.

Second, attributing the food to them implies that it became food through their action. This properly applies only to slaughtered animals, which become meat through their ritual slaughter. As for fruits, Allah created them edible; they did not become food through any human action.

Third, He coupled the lawfulness of their food with the lawfulness of marrying their women, and permitted our food to them just as He permitted their food to us. It is well known that the ruling concerning women applies specifically to the People of the Book, not to polytheists; the same therefore applies to the ruling concerning food. The ruling concerning fruits and grains, however, is not specific to the People of the Book.

Fourth, the word “food” is general in scope. Its inclusion of meat and the like is more evident than its inclusion of fruit, so the wording must retain its general scope, especially since it is coupled with Allah Most High’s words:

“And your food is lawful for them.”

We may feed them every kind of our food; likewise, we may eat every kind of their food. Furthermore, it is established in the authentic hadith collections—indeed, through widely transmitted reports:

“The Jewish woman presented the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, with a roasted sheep in the year of Khaybar. He ate a morsel of it, then said:

---

## Page 16661

‘This sheep tells me that it contains poison.’” Had the animals they slaughtered not been lawful to eat, he would not have eaten any of that sheep.

It is also established in an authentic hadith:

“When they went on the expedition to Khaybar, one of the Companions took a leather bag containing fat. He said, ‘I said, “I will not share any of this with anyone today.” Then I turned around, and there was the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, laughing.’ He did not rebuke him.”

Scholars have cited this as evidence that the Muslim army may eat the food of those at war with them before the spoils are divided.

Furthermore:

“The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, accepted a Jew’s invitation to a meal of barley bread and rancid rendered fat.”

This was narrated by Imam Ahmad. Ihālah (rendered fat) is fat obtained from a slaughtered animal, such as clarified butter and the like, which is ordinarily found in their cooking vessels. Had the animals they slaughtered been forbidden to eat, their vessels would have been like those of the Magians and others like them. It is established that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace,

“forbade eating from their vessels, then permitted it provided they were washed.”

Moreover, it is widely reported that when the Companions of the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, conquered Syria, Iraq, and Egypt, they ate animals slaughtered by the People of the Book—Jews and Christians—and abstained only from those slaughtered by the Magians.

The cheese made by the Magians gave rise to the well-known disagreement among Muslims, because cheese requires rennet, and scholars have a well-known disagreement concerning rennet from an animal that has not been lawfully slaughtered. Abū Hanīfah holds that it is ritually pure, whereas Mālik and al-Shāfiʿī hold that it is ritually impure. Two opinions are reported from Ahmad.

---

## Page 16662

Section: The Second Ground

The second ground for objecting to those who eat animals slaughtered by the People of the Book is that it is not known whether those living today descend from people who embraced their religion before its abrogation and alteration. This is the ground indicated by the questioner’s words, and it is a matter over which Muslim scholars of Ahl al-Sunnah wa-l-Jamā‘ah have differed. It rests on a fundamental question concerning the words of Allah Most High:

“The food of those who were given the Scripture is lawful for you, and your food is lawful for them. And lawful to you are chaste women from among the believers and chaste women from among those who were given the Scripture before you.”

Does this mean anyone who, after the revelation of the Quran, follows the religion of the People of the Book? Or does it mean someone whose ancestors embraced the religion of the People of the Book before its abrogation and alteration? Scholars hold two views on this.

The first view is held by the majority of Muslims of both the early and later generations. It is the position of Abū Hanīfah and Mālik, and one of the two views within Ahmad’s school; indeed, it is the position he explicitly stated.

The second is the position of al-Shāfi‘ī and a group of scholars within Ahmad’s school.

[The basis of this view is that ‘Alī and Ibn ‘Abbās disagreed concerning animals slaughtered by Banū Taghlib. ‘Alī said:

“Neither the animals they slaughter nor marriage to their women is lawful, for they have retained nothing of Christianity except drinking

---

## Page 16663

wine.” It is also reported that he said: “We shall wage war against them because they have not fulfilled the conditions imposed upon them by ‘Uthmān, who stipulated that they should] (1), along with other conditions.”

Ibn ‘Abbās, however, said that they are lawful, citing the words of Allah Most High:

“Whoever among you takes them as allies is indeed one of them.”

The general body of Muslims, including the Companions and others, did not prohibit the animals they slaughtered. Such a prohibition is known only from ‘Alī. A statement conveying the same meaning as Ibn ‘Abbās’s view has also been reported from ‘Umar ibn al-Khattāb.

Some scholars preferred the view of ‘Umar and Ibn ‘Abbās. This is the majority position, held by Abū Hanīfah, Mālik, and Ahmad according to one of the two views reported from him—a view that a group of scholars of his school judged correct. Indeed, it was his final position. Moreover, the general body of Muslims among the Companions, the Successors, and their followers held this view.

Abū Bakr al-Athram said: “I know of none of the Companions of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, who disapproved of it except ‘Alī.” This is the position of the overwhelming majority of the jurists of the Hijāz and Iraq, and of the jurists of both the hadith and reasoned-opinion traditions, such as al-Hasan, Ibrāhīm al-Nakha‘ī, al-Zuhrī, and others. It is also the position transmitted from Ahmad by most scholars of his school. Ibrāhīm ibn al-Hārith said: “Ahmad’s final position was that he saw nothing objectionable in the animals they slaughtered.”

Other scholars preferred ‘Alī’s view. This is the position of al-Shāfi‘ī and of Ahmad according to one of the two views reported from him. Ahmad’s reasoning differed only concerning Banū Taghlib, the very people concerning whom the Companions disagreed.

As for the other Jews and Christians among the Arabs, such as Tanūkh and Bahrā’

> The report from ‘Alī, may Allah be pleased with him, is confused here because of the poor condition of the original—and Allah knows best. The conditions imposed on Banū Taghlib were laid down by ‘Umar, not ‘Uthmān—may Allah be pleased with them both.
